. Similar to the case of the mass-critical gKdV equation (see
e.g.

[
35
, Section 3.1]
), for all
(i,j)\in\left\{1,2\right\}^{2}
, we define the following energy functionals of
\varepsilon
,

\mathcal{F}_{i,j}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\psi_{B}+(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j})\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{i,B}-\frac{1}{2}\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{4}-Q_{b}^{4}-4Q_{b}^{3}\varepsilon)\big){\rm d}y.

(4.2)

The following qualitative estimate of the time variation of
\mathcal{F}_{i,j}
plays an important role in our analysis (see more details in Section
4.3
and Section
5
).

Proposition 4.1
.

There exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<\min\left\{\kappa^{*},B^{-100}\right\}
small enough such that the following holds. Assume that for all
s\in[0,s_{0}]
, the solution
u(t)
with initial data
u_{0}
satisfies the bootstrap assumption (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) with
0<\kappa<\kappa_{1}
. Then for all
(i,j)\in\{1,2\}^{2}
and
s\in[0,s_{0}]
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{i,j}}{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y

(4.3)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+C_{1}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{0}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{1}=C_{1}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

To complete the proof of Proposition
4.1
, we first recall the following weighted Sobolev estimates on
\mathbb{R}
introduced in
[
40
]
.

Lemma 4.2

(
[
40
]
)
.

Let
\omega:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
be a
C^{1}
function such that
\|\omega^{\prime}/\omega\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim 1
. Then, for all
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|f^{2}\sqrt{\omega}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|f^{\prime}|^{2}+|f|^{2}\right)\omega{\rm d}r\right),

\displaystyle\left\|f^{2}\sqrt{\omega}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|f^{\prime}|^{2}\omega{\rm d}r+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f|^{2}\omega\left(\frac{\omega^{\prime}}{\omega}\right)^{2}{\rm d}r\right).

Proof.

The proof relies on a standard argument based on the Fundamental Theorem and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality (see
e.g.

[
40
, Lemma 6]
), and we omit it.
∎

Next, we generalize the above 1D weighted Sobolev estimate to the case of 2D.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\omega:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
be a
C^{2}
function such that

\left\|\frac{\nabla\omega}{\omega}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\omega}{\omega}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim 1.

(4.4)

Then, for all
f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\|f^{2}\sqrt{\omega}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|f|^{2}+|\nabla f|^{2})\sqrt{\omega}{\rm d}y\right)^{2}

(4.5)

\displaystyle+\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial_{y_{1}}\partial_{y_{2}}f|^{2}\omega{\rm d}y\right).

Moreover, for all
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we have

\|f^{2}\sqrt{\omega}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla f|^{2}+|f|^{2})\omega{\rm d}y\right).

(4.6)

Proof.

First, by an elementary computation, we see that

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{1}}\partial_{y_{2}}\left(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega}\right)

\displaystyle=f\sqrt{\omega}\left(\partial_{y_{1}}f\frac{\partial_{y_{2}}\omega}{\omega}+\partial_{y_{2}}f\frac{\partial_{y_{1}}\omega}{\omega}\right)+f^{2}\sqrt{\omega}\frac{\partial_{y_{1}}\partial_{y_{2}}\omega}{2\omega}

\displaystyle+2\left(\partial_{y_{1}}f\partial_{y_{2}}f+f\partial_{y_{1}}\partial_{y_{2}}f\right)\sqrt{\omega}-f^{2}\sqrt{\omega}\left(\frac{\partial_{y_{1}}\omega}{2\omega}\right)\left(\frac{\partial_{y_{2}}\omega}{2\omega}\right).

Combining the above identity with (
4.4
), the Fundamental Theorem and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we complete the proof of (
4.5
).

Second, using (
4.4
) and Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega})(\cdot,y_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f(\cdot,y_{2})\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|\partial_{y_{1}}f(\rho,y_{2})|^{2}+|f(\rho,y_{2})|^{2}\right)\omega(\rho,y_{2}){\rm d}\rho,

\displaystyle\left\|(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega})(y_{1},\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f(y_{1},\cdot)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|\partial_{y_{2}}f(y_{1},\rho)|^{2}+|f(y_{1},\rho)|^{2}\right)\omega(y_{1},\rho){\rm d}\rho.

It follows from the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\|(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega})(\cdot,y_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}{\rm d}y_{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\|(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega})(y_{1},\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}{\rm d}y_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla f(y)|^{2}+|f(y)|^{2}\right)\omega(y){\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Based on the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f^{4}(y)\omega(y){\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\|(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega})(\cdot,y_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\left\|(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega})(y_{1},\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\|(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega})(\cdot,y_{2})\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}{\rm d}y_{2}\right)\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\|(f^{2}\sqrt{\omega})(y_{1},\cdot)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}{\rm d}y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla f(y)|^{2}+|f(y)|^{2}\right)\omega{\rm d}y\right),

which completes the proof of (
4.6
).
∎

From the definitions of
\varphi_{i,B}
and
\psi_{B}
, we can easily check that, for
i=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\nabla\psi_{B}}{\psi_{B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\|\frac{\nabla\varphi_{i,B}}{\varphi_{i,B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\|\frac{\nabla\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}}{\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left(\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\psi_{B}}{\psi_{B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\varphi_{i,B}}{\varphi_{i,B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}}{{\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim 1.

On the other hand, using again the definitions of
\varphi_{i,B}
and
\psi_{B}
, the Hölder inequality and the bootstrap assumption (
3.6
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}y_{1}^{7}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{70}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}y_{1}^{100}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{94}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}y_{1}^{6}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\right)^{\frac{93}{94}}

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{70}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y+\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{50}{47}}}\right)\left(B^{70}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}\varphi^{\prime}_{1,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\right)^{\frac{93}{94}},

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}y_{1}^{8}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{80}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}y_{1}^{100}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{93}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}y_{1}^{7}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\right)^{\frac{92}{93}}

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{80}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y+\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{100}{93}}}\right)\left(B^{80}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}\varphi^{\prime}_{2,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\right)^{\frac{92}{93}}.

We now give a complete proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

For all
(i,j)\in\left\{1,2\right\}^{2}
, we decompose

\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{i,j}}{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}\right)=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{F}_{i,j}-\theta(j-1)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\mathcal{F}_{i,j}=\mathcal{I}_{1}+\mathcal{I}_{2}+\mathcal{I}_{3},

(4.10)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\partial_{s}\varepsilon-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon\right)\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}\left((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\right)\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}=2\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\partial_{s}\varepsilon-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon\right)\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon{\rm d}y-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\partial_{s}Q_{b}\left(3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}=2\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda\varepsilon\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j})\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}\left((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\right)\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y-\theta(j-1)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\mathcal{F}_{i,j}.

Step 1.
Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1}
. We claim that, there exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<B^{-100}
small enough, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y

(4.11)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{2}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+{C_{3}}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{2}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{3}=C_{3}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Indeed, we use (
3.8
) to rewrite

\partial_{s}\varepsilon-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon=\partial_{y_{1}}\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}+Q_{b}^{3}\right)+{\rm{Mod}}-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\Lambda\varepsilon+\Psi_{b}.

Based on the above identity, we decompose

\mathcal{I}_{1}=\mathcal{I}_{1,1}+\mathcal{I}_{1,2}+\mathcal{I}_{1,3},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Psi_{b}\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}\left((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\right)\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{y_{1}}\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}+Q_{b}^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}\left((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\right)\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,3}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left({\rm{Mod}}-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\Lambda\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}\left((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\right)\right){\rm d}y.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}
.
Note that, from Lemma
2.3
, the definition of
\varphi_{i,B}
and
\psi_{B}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\Psi_{b}|^{2}+\left|\Psi_{b}\right|^{2}\right)\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}+|y_{1}|^{2}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}\mathbf{1}_{[B^{10},\infty)}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim|b|^{\frac{7}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{-|b|^{-\frac{4}{3}}}e^{\frac{y_{1}}{B}}e^{-\frac{|y_{2}|}{3}}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}+b^{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{|y|}{3}}\left(1+|y_{1}|^{10}\right){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}

\displaystyle+b^{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{\frac{y_{1}}{B}}e^{-\frac{|y_{2}|}{3}}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\lesssim Bb^{4}.

It follows from (
3.4
), (
4.7
), Lemma
2.3
, Lemma
3.4
and Lemma
4.3
that,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{\frac{1}{2}}b^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(4.12)

\displaystyle+|b|^{\frac{7}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+|b|^{\frac{7}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{4}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{4}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{5}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{4}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{5}=C_{5}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1,2}
.
By an elementary computation, we decompose

\mathcal{I}_{1,2}=\mathcal{I}_{1,2,1}+\mathcal{I}_{1,2,2}+\mathcal{I}_{1,2,3},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,1}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\partial_{y_{1}}\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon\right)\right)\left(-\psi^{\prime}_{B}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon+\left(\varphi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}\right)\varepsilon\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,2}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\partial_{y_{1}}\left((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q_{b}^{3}\right)\right)\left(\psi^{\prime}_{B}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon-\left(\varphi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}\right)\varepsilon\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,3}

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\partial_{y_{1}}\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}+Q_{b}^{3}\right)\right)\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}+Q_{b}^{3}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y.

From integration by parts, we directly deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,1}=

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi^{\prime}_{B}\left((\partial^{2}_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y_{1}}\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}\right){\rm d}y-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}\left(3\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}-\psi^{\prime}_{B}-\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\left((\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}-\psi^{\prime}_{B})-(\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i,B}-\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B})\right){\rm d}y-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}-\psi^{\prime}_{B}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,2}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\varphi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}\right)(\partial_{y_{1}}Q_{b})\left(3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}-\psi^{\prime}_{B}\right)(6Q^{2}_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+8Q_{b}\varepsilon^{3}+3\varepsilon^{4}){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+6\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\psi^{\prime}_{B}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\right)\left((\partial_{y_{1}}Q_{b})(2Q_{b}\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2})+(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{2}\right){\rm d}y,\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,3}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi^{\prime}_{B}\bigg(2|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\sum_{|\alpha|=2}|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\varepsilon|^{2}\bigg){\rm d}y+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}(\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}-\left(\psi^{\prime}_{B}-\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}+Q_{b}^{3}\right)^{2}-\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon\right)^{2}\right)\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y.

Based on the above identities, we rewrite the term
\mathcal{I}_{1,2}
by

\mathcal{I}_{1,2}=\mathcal{I}_{1,2,2}+\mathcal{I}_{1,2,4}+\mathcal{I}_{1,2,5}+\mathcal{I}_{1,2,6},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,4}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi^{\prime}_{B}\left(3(\partial^{2}_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}+4(\partial_{y_{1}}\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}+(\partial^{2}_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,5}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}\left(3\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi^{\prime}_{B}-\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi^{\prime}_{B}-\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}\right){\rm d}y-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\left(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}-\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i,B}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,6}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon-(Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}+Q_{b}^{3}\right)^{2}-\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon\right)^{2}\right)\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y.

First, from (
3.4
), (
4.7
), Lemma
3.4
, Lemma
3.5
and Lemma
4.3
, we have

\mathcal{I}_{1,2,2}\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi^{\prime}_{B}\varepsilon^{2}(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}{\rm d}y.

Note that, using again (
4.7
) and Lemma
4.3
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y\right)

\displaystyle+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y\right)\left(\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\varepsilon\right|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By integration by parts, (
4.7
) and Lemma
3.4
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\left(B^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+B^{-\frac{1}{2}}\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon\Delta\varepsilon\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\varepsilon\right|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y.

It follows from (
3.4
) that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi^{\prime}_{B}\varepsilon^{2}(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}{\rm d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\varepsilon\right|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y

(4.13)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{B^{30}}\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\varepsilon\right|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y.

Second, from the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{1,2,5}
and Lemma
3.4
, we directly have

\mathcal{I}_{1,2,5}+\frac{9}{10}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y.

(4.14)

Based on a similar argument to the one in the estimate of
\mathcal{I}_{1,2,2}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2,6}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y

(4.15)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{B^{30}}\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\varepsilon\right|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y.

Here, we use the fact that

\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\varepsilon\right|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y+\mathcal{N}_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\sqrt{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\rm d}y.

Combining estimates (
4.13
)–(
4.15
) with the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{1,2,4}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{8}{9}\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{\alpha}_{y}\varepsilon|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y

(4.16)

\displaystyle-\frac{8}{9}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y+\frac{C_{6}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y.

Here,
C_{6}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{1,3}
.
By an elementary computation, we decompose

\mathcal{I}_{1,3}=\mathcal{I}_{1,3,1}+\mathcal{I}_{1,3,2}+\mathcal{I}_{1,3,3}+\mathcal{I}_{1,3,4}+\mathcal{I}_{1,3,5},

where

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,1}=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\widetilde{\rm{Mod}}\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right){\rm d}y,\quad\quad\quad\quad\

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,2}=2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda Q\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right){\rm d}y,\ \

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,3}=2\left(\frac{x_{1s}}{\lambda}-1\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{y_{1}}Q\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right){\rm d}y,\

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,4}=2\left(\frac{x_{1s}}{\lambda}-1\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right){\rm d}y,\

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,5}=2\frac{x_{2s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{2}}Q+\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right){\rm d}y.\

Here, we denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rm{Mod}}

\displaystyle={\rm{Mod}}-\left(\frac{x_{1s}}{\lambda}-1\right)(\partial_{y_{1}}Q+\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle-\frac{x_{2s}}{\lambda}(\partial_{y_{2}}Q+\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\left(\Lambda Q+\Lambda\varepsilon\right).

Indeed, by integration by parts, we deduce that,

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,1}=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\left(\psi_{B}\nabla{\widetilde{\rm{Mod}}}\right)\varepsilon{\rm d}y+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\widetilde{\rm{Mod}}\left(\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right){\rm d}y.

Based on Lemma
3.5
and Lemma
3.6
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{{\rm{Mod}}}\right|+\left|\nabla\widetilde{{\rm{Mod}}}\right|+\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\widetilde{{\rm{Mod}}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left(e^{-\frac{|y_{2}|}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{[-2,0]}(|b|^{\frac{3}{4}}y_{1})+e^{-\frac{|y|}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,\infty)}(y_{1})\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right).

Therefore, from (
4.7
), Lemma
3.4
and Lemma
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,3,1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-\frac{|y_{2}|}{4}}|\varepsilon|\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}

(4.17)

\displaystyle+\left(b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{|y|}{4}}|\varepsilon|\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}

\displaystyle+\left(b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+\left(b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{4}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{7}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+C_{8}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{7}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{8}=C_{8}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
.

By direct computations, we check the following identity

\displaystyle 2\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right)

(4.18)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\varepsilon+2(\varphi_{i,B}-1)\varepsilon-\left(3(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon+3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right)

\displaystyle+(2\psi_{B}-1)\left(-\Delta\varepsilon-3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon-3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}-\varepsilon^{3}\right)-2\psi^{\prime}_{B}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon.

From the orthogonality condition (
3.3
), (
4.18
) and integration by parts,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,3,2}

\displaystyle=-2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda Q(2\psi_{B}-1)\left(3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon+3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda Q\left(2(\varphi_{i,B}-1)\varepsilon-(2\psi_{B}-1)\Delta\varepsilon-2\psi^{\prime}_{B}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle-2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda Q(3(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon+3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}){\rm d}y.

Then, from the definition of
\varphi_{i,B}
, Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
3.4
,

\displaystyle|\Lambda Q|\left(|2\psi_{B}-1|+|2\varphi_{i,B}-1|+|\psi^{\prime\prime}_{B}|\right)+|\partial_{y_{1}}\Lambda Q||\psi^{\prime}_{B}|\lesssim e^{-\frac{1}{6}B^{\frac{1}{3}}}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}\psi_{B}.

It follows from Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
4.3
that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda Q(2\psi_{B}-1)\left(3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon+3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda Q\left(2(\varphi_{i,B}-1)\varepsilon-(2\psi_{B}-1)\Delta\varepsilon-2\psi^{\prime}_{B}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(b^{2}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left(|b|+e^{-\frac{1}{6}B^{\frac{1}{3}}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}\left(|\varepsilon|+|\varepsilon|^{4}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{9}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{10}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{9}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{10}=C_{10}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
. Next, using again (
3.4
), Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Lambda Q(3(Q_{b}^{2}-Q^{2})\varepsilon+3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(b^{2}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}\left(|b||\varepsilon|+\varepsilon^{2}+|\varepsilon|^{3}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{11}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{12}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{11}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{12}=C_{12}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
.

Combining the above estimates, we deduce that

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,2}\leq\frac{C_{13}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{14}b^{4}.

(4.19)

Here,
C_{13}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{14}=C_{14}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
.

Based on a similar argument to the one in the estimate of
\mathcal{I}_{1,3,2}
, we deduce that

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,3}\leq\frac{C_{15}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+C_{16}b^{4}.

(4.20)

Here,
C_{15}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{16}=C_{16}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
.

Note also that, by integration by parts,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\psi^{\prime}_{B}+\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}-3\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}(Q^{2}_{b}\psi_{B})-2\varepsilon^{3}\partial_{y_{1}}(Q_{b}\psi_{B})-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{4}\psi^{\prime}_{B}\right){\rm d}y.

It follows from (
3.4
), Lemma
3.5
and Lemma
4.3
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\left(-\nabla\cdot(\psi_{B}\nabla\varepsilon)+\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon-\psi_{B}((Q_{b}+\varepsilon)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b})\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+b^{4}.

Therefore, from (
3.6
), we obtain

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,4}\leq\frac{C_{17}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+C_{18}b^{4}.

(4.21)

Here,
C_{17}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{18}=C_{18}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
.

Based on a similar argument to the one in the estimate of
\mathcal{I}_{1,3,2}
and
\mathcal{I}_{1,3,4}
, we deduce that

\mathcal{I}_{1,3,5}\leq\frac{C_{19}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+C_{20}b^{4}.

(4.22)

Here,
C_{19}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{20}=C_{20}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
.

Combining estimates (
4.17
)–(
4.22
), we conclude that

\mathcal{I}_{1,3}\leq\frac{C_{21}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+C_{22}b^{4}.

(4.23)

Here,
C_{21}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{22}=C_{22}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
.

We see that (
4.11
) follows from (
4.12
), (
4.16
) and (
4.23
).

Step 2.
Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{2}
. We claim that, there exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<B^{-100}
small enough, such that

\mathcal{I}_{2}\leq\frac{C_{23}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{24}b^{4}.

(4.24)

Here,
C_{23}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{24}=C_{24}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Indeed, using Lemma
3.2
and integration by parts,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left({\rm{Mod}}-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\Lambda\varepsilon\right)\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon{\rm d}y+2\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Psi_{b}\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon{\rm d}y

\displaystyle-\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}-\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i,B})\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y-\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}\left(3(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\varphi_{i,B}\partial_{y_{1}}Q_{b}+\psi_{B}\partial_{s}Q_{b})\left(3Q_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}\left(6Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}+8Q_{b}\varepsilon^{3}+3\varepsilon^{4}\right).

On the other hand, from the definition of
\mathcal{J}_{i,j}
and (
3.4
),

|\mathcal{J}_{i,j}|\lesssim J_{1}\lesssim\left(B^{10}\mathcal{N}_{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim(B^{10}\kappa)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore, using a similar argument to the one in Step 1, we complete the proof of (
4.24
) by taking
\kappa
small enough and
B
large enough.

Step 3.
Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{3}
. We claim that, there exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<B^{-100}
small enough, such that

\mathcal{I}_{3}\leq\frac{C_{25}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{26}b^{4}.

(4.25)

Here,
C_{25}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{26}=C_{26}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Indeed, by an elementary computation, we decompose

\mathcal{I}_{3}=\mathcal{I}_{3,1}+\mathcal{I}_{3,2}+\mathcal{I}_{3,3}+\mathcal{I}_{3,4},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,1}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\Lambda Q_{b}\left(3Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left(2-\theta(j-1)\right)\psi_{B}-y_{1}\psi^{\prime}_{B}\right)|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}{\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,3}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left(2-\theta(j-1)\right)\psi_{B}-y_{1}\psi^{\prime}_{B}\right)(6Q^{2}_{b}\varepsilon^{2}+4Q_{b}\varepsilon^{3}+\varepsilon^{4}){\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}-\theta(j-1)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j})\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}y_{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{3,1}
.

Using (
3.4
), (
4.7
), Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|b|+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\left(\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{4}\right){\rm d}y

(4.26)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{27}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+C_{28}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{27}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{28}=C_{28}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{3,2}
.

Using (
3.4
), (
4.7
), (iv) of Lemma
3.4
and (ii) of Lemma
3.6
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|b|+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y

(4.27)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{29}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{30}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{29}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{30}=C_{30}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{3,3}
.

Using a similar argument to the one above, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|b|+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{4}\right)(\sqrt{\psi_{B}}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y

(4.28)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{31}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{32}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{31}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{32}=C_{32}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Estimate on
\mathcal{I}_{3,4}
.
According to the integration region, we decompose

\mathcal{I}_{3,4}=\mathcal{I}_{3,4,1}+\mathcal{I}_{3,4,2}+\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{B^{10}}y_{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}-\theta(j-1)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j})\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{B^{10}}\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}y_{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}-\theta(j-1)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j})\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}\varphi_{i,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}.

Note that, from (
3.4
), the definition of
\mathcal{J}_{i,j}
and Lemma
3.7
,

\left|\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right|+\left|\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right|\lesssim|J_{1s}|+|J_{1}|\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}+B^{5}\kappa+B^{5}\kappa^{2}\lesssim B^{-50}.

Note also that, for any
B
large enough and
y_{1}<B^{10}
, we have

|y_{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}|\lesssim|B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}|^{\frac{99}{100}}.

It follows from (
3.4
), (iv) of Lemma
3.4
, Lemma
3.6
and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,1}

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{\frac{99}{100}}\|\varepsilon\|^{\frac{1}{100}}_{L^{2}}\left(|b|+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{99}{100}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{33}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{34}b^{4},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y.

Here,
C_{33}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{34}=C_{34}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

On the other hand, using the definition of
\varphi_{i,B}
,

(i+6)\varphi_{i,B}(y_{1})-y_{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}(y_{1})=\frac{\psi^{\prime}_{B}(y_{1})}{\sqrt{2\psi_{B}(y_{1})}}\left(\frac{y_{1}}{B^{10}}\right)^{i+6},\quad\mbox{for}\ y_{1}\geq B^{10}.

Based on the above identity, we rewrite the term
\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3}
by

\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3}=\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3,1}+\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3,2},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{i+6}\left(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}-\left(\theta(j-1)+i+6\right)\left(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}y_{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{i+6}\left(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}-\theta(j-1)\left(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{B^{10}}^{\infty}\frac{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\sqrt{2\psi_{B}}}\left(\frac{y_{1}}{B^{10}}\right)^{i+6}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}.

Note that, from the definition of
\mathcal{J}_{i,j}
and an elementary computation,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}-\left(\theta(j-1)+i+6\right)\left(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\left(\theta(j-1)+i+6\right)\left(1-J_{1}\right)^{-2\theta(j-1)-2i-13}\left(2J_{1s}-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}J_{1}\right).

It follows from (
3.4
), Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
3.7
that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}-\left(\theta(j-1)+i+6\right)\left(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|b|+B^{5}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(B^{10}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y.

Using (
3.4
) and
\theta>\frac{8}{5}>\frac{100}{93}>\frac{50}{47}
, we have

(|b|+\mathcal{N}_{2})\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\theta}}\right)\leq 2\kappa\Longrightarrow(|b|+\mathcal{N}_{2})\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{8}{5}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{50}{47}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{93}{100}}}\right)\leq 8\kappa.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\frac{|b|}{\lambda^{\frac{50}{47}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|b|^{\frac{63}{188}}\kappa^{\frac{125}{188}},\quad\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}}{\lambda^{\frac{50}{47}}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\frac{63}{188}}\kappa^{\frac{125}{188}},

\displaystyle\frac{|b|}{\lambda^{\frac{100}{93}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|b|^{\frac{61}{186}}\kappa^{\frac{125}{186}},\quad\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}}{\lambda^{\frac{100}{93}}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{2}^{\frac{61}{186}}\kappa^{\frac{125}{186}}.

Next, using again the definition of
\varphi_{i,B}
, we find

\left|y_{1}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}\right|\lesssim B^{-10(i+6)}y_{1}^{i+6},\quad\mbox{on}\ y_{1}>B^{10}.

Therefore, for
i=1
, from (
4.8
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|b|+B^{5}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(B^{10}\varphi^{\prime}_{1,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{50}{47}}}\right)\left(|b|+B^{5}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(B^{10}\varphi^{\prime}_{1,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi^{\prime}_{1,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{93}{94}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{1,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+b^{4}.

Similarly, for
i=2
, from (
4.9
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(|b|+B^{5}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(B^{10}\varphi^{\prime}_{2,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\psi_{B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda^{\frac{100}{93}}}\right)\left(|b|+B^{5}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(B^{10}\varphi^{\prime}_{2,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi^{\prime}_{2,B}\varepsilon^{2}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{92}{93}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{2,B}+\psi_{B}\right){\rm d}y+b^{4}.

On the other hand, using again (
3.4
), Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
3.7
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\mathcal{J}_{i,j}-\theta(j-1)\left(1+\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right)\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|b|+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+B^{5}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(B^{10}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y.

Moreover, from the definition of
\psi_{B}
, we see that

\left|\frac{\psi^{\prime}_{B}}{\sqrt{2\psi_{B}}}\left(\frac{y_{1}}{B^{10}}\right)^{i+6}\right|=\frac{1}{B}\exp\left(-2\left(\frac{y_{1}}{B^{\frac{2}{3}}}+\frac{1}{3}B^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)\right)\left(\frac{y_{1}}{B^{10}}\right)^{i+6}\lesssim B^{-30}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}.

It follows that

\mathcal{I}_{3,4,3,2}\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\mathcal{I}_{3,4}\leq\frac{C_{35}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{B}){\rm d}y+C_{36}b^{4}.

(4.29)

Here,
C_{35}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{36}=C_{36}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

We see that (
4.25
) follows from (
4.26
), (
4.27
), (
4.28
) and (
4.29
).

Step 4.
Conclusion. Combining the estimates (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.24
) and (
4.25
), we complete the proof of (
4.3
) by taking
B
large enough.
∎

4.2.
Virial estimate

In this subsection, we introduce the virial estimate related to the solution of (
1.1
). As we mentioned in §
1.3
, since the lack of coercivity of the Schrödinger operator appears in the primal virial estimate of
\varepsilon
, we should first introduce a transformed problem, and then based on the special structure of the transformed linearized problem and numerical computation, we could obtain the coercivity and virial estimates for this transformed problem (see more details in Proposition
4.9
, Lemma
4.10
and
[
9
, Lemma 14.2 and §16]
).

We define the smooth function
\sigma\in[0,1]
as follows,

\sigma(y_{1})=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ for }|y_{1}|>2,\\
1,&\text{ for }|y_{1}|\leq 1.\end{cases}

Moreover, we define the smooth function
\psi_{0}\in(0,1]
as follows,

\psi_{0}(y_{1})=\begin{cases}e^{6y_{1}},&\text{ for }y_{1}<-1,\\
\frac{1}{2},&\text{ for }y_{1}>-\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}\quad\mbox{with}\ \ \psi_{0}^{\prime}(y_{1})\geq 0,\ \ \mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

We also define the smooth function
\psi_{1}\in(0,\infty)
as follows,

\psi_{1}(y_{1})=\begin{cases}e^{10y_{1}},&\text{ for }y_{1}<-1,\\
1+y_{1},&\text{ for }y_{1}>-\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}\quad\mbox{with}\ \ \psi_{1}^{\prime}(y_{1})>0,\ \ \mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

Let
B>100
be a large enough universal constant to be chosen later. We set

\psi_{0,B}(y_{1})=\psi_{0}\left(\frac{y_{1}}{B}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\psi_{1,B}(y_{1})=\psi_{1}\left(\frac{y_{1}}{B}\right).

We also set

\chi_{B}(y_{1})=\begin{cases}\sigma\left(\frac{y_{1}}{2B}\right)\int_{0}^{y_{1}}\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}(\rho){\rm d}\rho,\quad\quad\mbox{for}\ y_{1}\leq 0,\\
\sigma\left(\frac{y_{1}}{10B^{10}}\right)\int_{0}^{y_{1}}\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}(\rho){\rm d}\rho,\quad\mbox{for}\ y_{1}>0.\end{cases}

By the definition of the weight functions, we have the following pointwise estimate.

Lemma 4.4
.

The following estimates hold.

(i)

Estimates on
\varphi_{i,B}
.
For
i=1,2
, we have

\displaystyle|\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{i,B}|

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{-\frac{2}{3}}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+B^{-20}\psi_{0,B},\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R},

\displaystyle|\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{i,B}|

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{-\frac{4}{3}}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+B^{-30}\psi_{0,B},\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

(ii)

Estimates on
\psi_{0,B}
and
\chi_{B}
.
We have

\displaystyle B|\chi^{\prime}_{B}|+B^{2}|\chi^{\prime\prime}_{B}|+B^{3}\left|\chi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\psi_{0,B},\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R},

\displaystyle B\left|\psi^{\prime}_{0,B}\right|+B^{2}\left|\psi^{\prime\prime}_{0,B}\right|+B^{3}|\psi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0,B}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\psi_{0,B},\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

(iii)

First-type estimates on
\chi_{B}
.
We have

\chi_{B}\lesssim\min\left(B^{9}\psi_{0,B},\psi_{1,B}\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right),\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

(iv)

Second-type estimates on
\chi_{B}
.
We have

\left|\chi^{\prime}_{B}-\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right|\lesssim B^{9}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B},\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

(v)

Third-type estimates on
\chi_{B}
.
We have

\displaystyle\left|\chi^{\prime}_{B}-\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{\left(-\infty,-\frac{B}{2}\right]}(y_{1})+\mathbf{1}_{\left[B^{10},\infty\right)}(y_{1}),\quad\quad\quad\ \ \mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R},

\displaystyle\left|\chi_{B}-\frac{2y_{1}}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathbf{1}_{\left(-\infty,-\frac{B}{2}\right]}(y_{1})+\mathbf{1}_{\left[B^{10},\infty\right)}(y_{1})\right)|y_{1}|,\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

Proof.

The proof is directly based on a similar argument to the proof for Lemma
3.3
–
3.5
and the definitions of
\chi_{B}
and
\psi_{0,B}
, and we omit it.
∎

Let
0<\gamma\ll 1
be a small enough constant (depending on
B
) to be chosen later. For any
s\in[0,s_{0})
, we set

\eta=\left(1-\gamma\Delta\right)^{-1}\mathcal{L}\varepsilon\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{P}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}(s,y)\chi_{B}(y_{1}){\rm d}y.

Note that, for any
\gamma>0
, we have

\left[Q^{2},(1-\gamma\Delta)\right]=\gamma\Delta(Q^{2})+2\gamma Q\nabla Q\cdot\nabla.

(4.30)

We denote

\displaystyle{\rm{Mod}}_{\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon+\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\left(\Lambda Q_{b}-\Lambda Q\right)

(4.31)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{x_{1s}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\partial_{y_{1}}Q_{b}-\partial_{y_{1}}Q+\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{x_{2s}}{\lambda}\left(\partial_{y_{2}}Q_{b}-\partial_{y_{2}}Q+\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon\right)-b_{s}\frac{\partial Q_{b}}{\partial{b}}.

We now state the equation and the orthogonality conditions of
\eta
.

Lemma 4.5

(Equation of
\eta
)
.

We have

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\eta

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}\partial_{y_{1}}\eta-3\gamma(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(\Delta(Q^{2})\partial_{y_{1}}\eta+2Q\nabla Q\cdot\nabla\partial_{y_{1}}\eta\right)

\displaystyle+(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}{\rm{Mod}}_{\eta}-2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}Q

\displaystyle+(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left({\mathcal{L}}\Psi_{b}-{\mathcal{L}}\partial_{y_{1}}R_{b}-{\mathcal{L}}\partial_{y_{1}}R_{NL}\right).

Moreover, the function
\eta
satisfies the following orthogonality conditions

\left(\eta,(1-\gamma\Delta)Q\right)=\left(\eta,(1-\gamma\Delta)\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)=\left(\eta,(1-\gamma\Delta)\partial_{y_{2}}Q\right)=0.

Proof.

The proof is based on (
3.3
), (
4.30
), (
4.31
), Proposition
2.1
, Lemma
3.2
and an elementary computation.
∎

In addition, based on the Fourier transform and elementary computations, we have the following identity related to
\varepsilon
and
\eta
.

Lemma 4.6
.

It holds

\displaystyle(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\mathcal{L}\Lambda\varepsilon-\Lambda\eta

\displaystyle=3(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(\varepsilon y\cdot\nabla\left(Q^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+2\gamma(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-2}\Delta\mathcal{L}\varepsilon-2(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta\varepsilon.

Proof.

First, we claim that, for any regular function
f
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\left[(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1},y\right]\cdot\nabla f=2\gamma\left(1-\gamma\Delta\right)^{-2}f.

(4.32)

Indeed, using the Fourier transform, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(\left[(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1},y\right]\cdot\nabla f\right)(\xi)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\xi}\cdot\left(\frac{\xi\widehat{f}(\xi)}{1+\gamma|\xi|^{2}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{1+\gamma|\xi|^{2}}\right)\nabla_{\xi}\cdot\left(\xi\widehat{f}(\xi)\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\xi}\left(\frac{1}{1+\gamma|\xi|^{2}}\right)\cdot\left(\xi\widehat{f}(\xi)\right)=-\frac{2\gamma|\xi|^{2}\widehat{f}(\xi)}{(1+\gamma|\xi|^{2})^{2}}=2\gamma\mathcal{F}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-2}\Delta f\right),

which implies (
4.32
). Moreover, from (
4.32
), we see that

y\cdot\nabla f-(1-\gamma\Delta)y\cdot\left(\nabla\left(1-\gamma\Delta\right)^{-1}f\right)=2\gamma(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta f.

(4.33)

Note that, from the definition of
\eta
, we have

\mathcal{L}\Lambda\varepsilon=(1-\gamma\Delta)\Lambda\eta-2\Delta\varepsilon+3\varepsilon y\cdot\nabla\left(Q^{2}\right)+y\cdot\nabla\mathcal{L}\varepsilon-(1-\gamma\Delta)(y\cdot\nabla\eta).

Using (
4.33
) and the definition of
\eta
, we deduce that

y\cdot\nabla\mathcal{L}\varepsilon-(1-\gamma\Delta)(y\cdot\nabla\eta)=2\gamma(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta\mathcal{L}\varepsilon.

Combining the above two identities, we complete the proof of Lemma
4.6
.
∎

Then, using the coercivity of
\mathcal{L}
, we obtain the following relations between
\varepsilon
and
\eta
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
B>100
be a large enough constant and
0<\gamma\ll 1
be a small enough constant. Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\gamma|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2})\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\gamma^{-1}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\gamma^{2}|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2})\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\gamma|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2})\psi_{1,B}{\rm d}y\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\gamma^{-1}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{1,B}{\rm d}y.

Here,
C>1
is a large enough universal constant independent of
B
and
\gamma
.

Proof.

First, from integration by parts, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)\eta\right)\eta\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\gamma|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y-\frac{\gamma}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi^{\prime\prime}_{0,B}{\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\mathcal{L}\varepsilon\right)\eta\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\nabla\varepsilon\cdot\nabla\eta+(1-3Q^{2})\varepsilon\eta\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)\eta\psi^{\prime}_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Combining the above identities with
B|\psi^{\prime}_{0,B}|+B^{2}|\psi^{\prime\prime}_{0,B}|\lesssim\psi_{0,B}
on
\mathbb{R}
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we complete the proof of the first estimate.

Next, using again integration by parts, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\mathcal{L}\varepsilon\right)\varepsilon\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{L}\left(\varepsilon\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right),\varepsilon\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi^{\prime\prime}_{0,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)\varepsilon\psi^{\prime}_{0,B}{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\frac{(\psi^{\prime}_{0,B})^{2}}{\psi_{0,B}}{\rm d}y.

It follows from (
3.3
), Proposition
2.1
and
B|\psi^{\prime}_{0,B}|+B^{2}|\psi^{\prime\prime}_{0,B}|\lesssim\psi_{0,B}
on
\mathbb{R}
that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)\eta\right)\varepsilon\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\mathcal{L}\varepsilon\right)\varepsilon\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\geq\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Combining the above estimate with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we complete the proof of the second estimate.

The proof of the third estimate is similar to the case of the first one.
∎

Based on a similar argument and Lemma
4.4
, we obtain the following estimate.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
B>100
be a large enough constant and
0<\gamma\ll 1
be a small enough constant. Then for all
i=1,2,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\gamma|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2})\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\gamma^{-1}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{B^{20}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Here,
C>1
is a large enough universal constant independent of
B
and
\gamma
.

We now state the virial estimate of
\eta
. Let
B>100
be a large enough constant to be chosen later and
\gamma=B^{-3}
. Then the following qualitative estimate of the time variation of
\mathcal{P}
is true.

Proposition 4.9
.

There exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough,
0<\kappa_{1}<\min\left\{\kappa^{*},B^{-100}\right\}
small enough and
0<\nu_{1}<1
small enough
(
independent of
B
)
such that the following holds. Assume that for all
s\in[0,s_{0}]
, the solution
u(t)
with initial data
u_{0}
satisfies the bootstrap assumption (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) with
0<\kappa<\kappa_{1}
. Then for all
(i,j)\in\left\{1,2\right\}^{2}
and
s\in[0,s_{0}]
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{\mathcal{P}}{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}\right)+\frac{\nu_{1}}{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

(4.34)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{37}}{B^{8}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B^{23}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y+C_{38}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{37}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{38}=C_{38}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

To complete the proof of Proposition
4.9
, we first recall the following coercivity result from
[
9
]
and the introduce a technical estimate related to the weighted norm.

For any
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}f=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}\partial_{y_{1}}^{2}f-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{y_{2}}^{2}f+\frac{1}{2}f-\left(\frac{3}{2}Q^{2}+3y_{1}Q\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)f

\displaystyle+3\frac{(f,y_{1}Q)}{(Q,Q)}Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q+3\frac{(f,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q)}{(Q,Q)}y_{1}Q.

We now recall the following coercivity result of
\mathcal{A}
from
[
9
]
.

Lemma 4.10

(
[
9
]
)
.

There exists
\nu_{2}>0
, such that for all
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,

\left(\mathcal{A}f,f\right)\geq\nu_{2}\|f\|^{2}_{H^{1}}-\frac{1}{\nu_{2}}\left(\left(f,Q\right)^{2}+\left(f,\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)^{2}+\left(f,\partial_{y_{2}}Q\right)^{2}\right).

Proof.

We refer to
[
9
, §16]
for the numerical checking of the coercivity result.
∎

Next we introduce the following weighted estimate.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\omega:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
be a
C^{2}
function such that

\left\|\frac{\nabla\omega}{\omega}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\omega}{\omega}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim 1.

(4.35)

Then, for all
f\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
k=0,1,2
, we have

\sum_{|\alpha|=k}\|\omega(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\partial_{y}^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\gamma^{-\frac{k}{2}}\|\omega f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Here,
C
is a universal constant independent of
\gamma
.

Proof.

First, from the Fourier transform and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\sum_{|\alpha|=k}\|(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}\rightarrow L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma^{-\frac{k}{2}},\quad\mbox{for all}\ k=0,1,2.

(4.36)

Second, for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
with
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
, we denote

F_{\alpha 1}=\omega(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\partial_{y}^{\alpha}f\quad\mbox{and}\quad F_{\alpha 2}=(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\omega\partial_{y}^{\alpha}f.

By an elementary computation, we find

\frac{1}{\omega}(1-\gamma\Delta)F_{\alpha 2}=\frac{1}{\omega}(1-\gamma\Delta)F_{\alpha 1}-\gamma F_{\alpha 1}\Delta\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)-2\gamma\nabla\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\cdot\nabla F_{\alpha 1},

which implies

\displaystyle F_{\alpha 1}

\displaystyle=F_{\alpha 2}-\gamma(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(\omega F_{\alpha 1}\Delta\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+2\gamma(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(\nabla\cdot\left(\omega F_{\alpha 1}\nabla\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle-2\gamma(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(F_{\alpha 1}\nabla\omega\cdot\nabla\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\right).

Next, from (
4.35
),

\omega\left|\Delta\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\right|+\omega\left|\nabla\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\right|+\left|\nabla\omega\cdot\nabla\left(\frac{1}{\omega}\right)\right|\lesssim 1.

It follows from (
4.36
) that

\|F_{\alpha 1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|F_{\alpha 2}\|_{L^{2}}+\gamma\|F_{\alpha 1}\|_{L^{2}}+\gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\|F_{\alpha 1}\|_{L^{2}}\Longrightarrow\|F_{\alpha 1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|F_{\alpha 2}\|_{L^{2}}.

On the other hand, for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
with
0\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
,

\omega\partial_{y}^{\alpha}f\in{\rm{Span}}\left\{\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{1}}(f\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{2}}\omega):\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}=\alpha\right\}.

It follows from (
4.35
) and (
4.36
) that

\left\|F_{\alpha 2}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}=\alpha}\left\|(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{1}}(f\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{2}}\omega)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha_{2}|\leq\alpha}\left\|f\partial_{y}^{\alpha_{2}}\omega\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\omega f\|_{L^{2}}.

Combining the above estimates, we complete the proof of Lemma
4.11
.
∎

Note that, the functions
\psi_{0,B}
and
\psi_{1,B}
satisfy

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\nabla\psi_{0,B}}{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\psi_{0,B}}{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1,

(4.37)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\nabla\psi_{1,B}}{\psi_{1,B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\psi_{1,B}}{\psi_{1,B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1.

We now give a complete proof of Proposition
4.9
.

Proof of Proposition
4.9
.

From Lemma
4.5
, we decompose

\frac{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{\mathcal{P}}{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}\mathcal{P}}{{\rm d}s}-\frac{\theta(j-1)}{2}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{2}+\mathcal{G}_{3}+\mathcal{G}_{4},

(4.38)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\mathcal{L}\Lambda\varepsilon\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y-\frac{\theta(j-1)}{2}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\mathcal{P},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2}

\displaystyle=-3\gamma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(\Delta(Q^{2})\partial_{y_{1}}\eta+2Q\nabla Q\cdot\nabla\partial_{y_{1}}\eta\right)\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\mathcal{L}\partial_{y_{1}}\eta\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y-2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}Q\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{4}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\mathcal{L}\left({\rm{Mod}_{\eta}}-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon+\Psi_{b}-\partial_{y_{1}}R_{b}-\partial_{y_{1}}R_{NL}\right)\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y.

Step 1.
Estimate on
\mathcal{G}_{1}
. We claim that, there exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<B^{-100}
small enough, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{39}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

(4.39)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{40}}{B^{30}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}){\rm d}y.

Here,
C_{39}>1
and
C_{40}>1
are some universal constants independent of
B
.

Indeed, from Lemma
4.6
and integration by parts, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\mathcal{L}\Lambda\varepsilon\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\chi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y+3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(\varepsilon y\cdot\nabla\left(Q^{2}\right)\right)\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+2\gamma\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-2}\Delta\mathcal{L}\varepsilon\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta\varepsilon\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y.

From Lemma
4.4
, we directly have

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\chi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Then, using again Lemma
4.4
, Lemma
4.11
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(\varepsilon y\cdot\nabla\left(Q^{2}\right)\right)\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{9}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{9}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y+B^{9}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Based on a similar argument, we also obtain

\displaystyle\gamma\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-2}\Delta\mathcal{L}\varepsilon\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y\right|+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta\varepsilon\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{12}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y+B^{12}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

On the other hand, using again Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.7
,

\left|\mathcal{P}\right|\lesssim B^{9}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\\
\lesssim B^{12}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

We see that (
4.39
) follows from the above estimates, (
3.4
) and (ii) of Lemma
3.6
.

Step 2.
Estimate on
\mathcal{G}_{2}
.
We claim that, there exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<B^{-100}
small enough, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{41}}{B^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

(4.40)

Here,
C_{41}>1
is a universal constants independent of
B
.

Indeed, using (
4.37
), Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.11
, we find

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(\Delta(Q^{2})\partial_{y_{1}}\eta\right)\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\||\nabla\eta|\Delta(Q^{2})\psi_{1,B}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Next, by an elementary computation,

2Q\nabla Q\cdot\nabla\partial_{y_{1}}\eta=2\partial_{y_{1}}\left(Q\nabla Q\cdot\nabla\eta\right)-2\nabla\eta\cdot\partial_{y_{1}}\left(Q\nabla Q\right).

It follows from (
4.37
), Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.11
that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\left(2Q\nabla Q\cdot\nabla\partial_{y_{1}}\eta\right)\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\left(Q\nabla Q\cdot\nabla\eta\right)\psi_{1,B}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\left(\nabla\eta\cdot\partial_{y_{1}}(Q\nabla Q)\right)\psi_{1,B}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left\|\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\eta|^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim B^{\frac{3}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

We see that (
4.40
) follows from the above estimates and
\gamma=B^{-3}
.

Step 3.
Estimate on
\mathcal{G}_{3}
. We claim that, there exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<B^{-100}
small enough, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\nu_{3}}{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

(4.41)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{42}}{B^{8}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B^{23}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}){\rm d}y+C_{43}b^{4}.

Here,
\nu_{3}>0
and
C_{42}>1
are some universal constants independent of
B
and
C_{43}=C_{43}(B)
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Indeed, by an elementary computation,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\mathcal{L}\partial_{y_{1}}\eta\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{y_{1}}\eta)^{2}\chi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\partial_{y_{2}}\eta\right)^{2}\chi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\chi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(Q\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)\eta^{2}\chi_{B}{\rm d}y+\frac{3}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q^{2}\eta^{2}\chi^{\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\chi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y.

Based on the above identity, we rewrite the term
\mathcal{G}_{3}
by

\mathcal{G}_{3}=\mathcal{G}_{3,1}+\mathcal{G}_{3,2}+\mathcal{G}_{3,3},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3,1}=\frac{6}{B}\frac{(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q)}{(Q,Q)}(\eta,y_{1}Q)-2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}Q,\eta\chi_{B}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3,2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(3(\partial_{y_{1}}\eta)^{2}+\left(\partial_{y_{2}}\eta\right)^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(Q^{2}+2y_{1}Q\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y-\frac{6}{B}\frac{(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q)}{(Q,Q)}(\eta,y_{1}Q),\qquad\qquad\quad

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3,3}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(3(\partial_{y_{1}}\eta)^{2}+\left(\partial_{y_{2}}\eta\right)^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\left(\chi^{\prime}_{B}-\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\chi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}Q^{2}\left(\chi^{\prime}_{B}-\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y+3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\left(Q\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)\left(\chi_{B}-\frac{2y_{1}}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y.

Estimate on
\mathcal{G}_{3,1}
.
Using again Lemma
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{L}\partial_{y_{1}}\eta,(1-\gamma\Delta)Q\right)

\displaystyle=2\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)(Q,Q)+\left(\mathcal{L}\partial_{y_{1}}R_{b}+\mathcal{L}\partial_{y_{1}}R_{NL},Q\right)

\displaystyle+3\gamma\left(\Delta(Q^{2})\partial_{y_{1}}\eta+2Q\nabla Q\cdot\nabla\partial_{y_{1}}\eta,Q\right)-\left(\mathcal{L}{\rm{Mod}}_{\eta}+\mathcal{L}\Psi_{b},Q\right).

Note that

\left(\mathcal{L}\partial_{y_{1}}\eta,(1-\gamma\Delta)Q\right)=6\left(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)+\gamma\left(\eta,\partial_{y_{1}}\mathcal{L}\Delta Q\right).

Therefore, from Lemma
3.6
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\left|\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b-3\frac{(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q)}{(Q,Q)}\right|\lesssim\gamma\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\eta|^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+b^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

It follows from (
3.4
) and Lemma
4.7
that

\displaystyle\left(\gamma\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+b^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left((\gamma+\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}})\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+b^{2}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{44}}{B^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+|\eta|^{2})\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y+C_{45}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{44}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{45}=C_{45}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
. In the above estimate, we use
\gamma=B^{-3}
.

Note also that

(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}Q=Q+\gamma(1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta Q.

Using (
4.37
), Lemma
4.4
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we find

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta Q\right)\eta\chi_{B}{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\psi_{1,B}\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta Q\right)\right|\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}{\rm d}y\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore, from
\chi_{B}(y_{1})=\frac{y_{1}}{B}
for
|y_{1}|\leq\frac{B}{2}
and
\gamma=B^{-3}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3,1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q)\left(\eta Q,\chi_{B}-\frac{y_{1}}{B}\right)\right|

(4.42)

\displaystyle+\left|\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b-3\frac{(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q)}{(Q,Q)}\right)\left(\eta Q,\chi_{B}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\gamma\left|\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\left((1-\gamma\Delta)^{-1}\Delta Q,\eta\chi_{B}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{46}}{B^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2})\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y+C_{47}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{46}>1
is a universal constant independent of
B
and
C_{47}=C_{47}(B)>1
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Estimate on
\mathcal{G}_{3,2}
.
By an elementary computation, we rewrite

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3,2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{B}\left(\mathcal{A}\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}},\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right)+\frac{3}{4B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{(\psi^{\prime}_{0,B})^{2}}{\psi_{0,B}}-2\psi^{\prime\prime}_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{6}{B(Q,Q)}\left(\left(\eta\sqrt{2\psi_{0,B}},y_{1}Q\right)\left(\eta\sqrt{2\psi_{0,B}},Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)-\left(\eta,y_{1}Q\right)\left(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)\right).

First, using Lemma
4.10
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{A}\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}},\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\nu_{2}\left\|\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-\frac{1}{\nu_{2}}\left(\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}},Q\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\nu_{2}}\left(\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}},\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{\nu_{2}}\left(\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}},\partial_{y_{2}}Q\right)^{2}.

On the one hand side, from Lemma
4.4
, we see that

\left\|\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}=\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{B}\right)\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left|\nabla\eta\right|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

On the other hand, from Lemma
4.5
and the definition of
\psi_{0,B}
,

\left(\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}},Q\right)^{2}+\left(\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}},\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)^{2}+\left(\eta\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}},\partial_{y_{2}}Q\right)^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{6}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Second, using again Lemma
4.4
, we have

\left|\frac{1}{B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{(\psi^{\prime}_{0,B})^{2}}{\psi_{0,B}}-2\psi^{\prime\prime}_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Last, using again the definition of
\psi_{0,B}
and the exponential decay of
Q
,

\displaystyle\left|\left(\eta\sqrt{2\psi_{0,B}},y_{1}Q\right)-\left(\eta,y_{1}Q\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(\eta\sqrt{2\psi_{0,B}},Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)-\left(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Based on the above estimates and the exponential decay of
Q
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left(\eta\sqrt{2\psi_{0,B}},y_{1}Q\right)\left(\eta\sqrt{2\psi_{0,B}},Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)-\left(\eta,y_{1}Q\right)\left(\eta,Q^{2}\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Combining the above estimates, for
B
large enough, we conclude that

\mathcal{G}_{3,2}\leq-\frac{\nu_{2}}{2B}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left|\nabla\eta\right|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

(4.43)

Estimate on
\mathcal{G}_{3,3}
.
Recall that, from Lemma
4.4
, we have

\left|\chi^{\prime}_{B}-\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right|\lesssim B^{9}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B},\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

It follows from Lemma
4.8
that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(3(\partial_{y_{1}}\eta)^{2}+\left(\partial_{y_{2}}\eta\right)^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\left(\chi^{\prime}_{B}-\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim B^{15}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{B^{8}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Next, using again Lemma
4.4
and the exponential decay of
Q
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\chi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{B}{\rm d}y\right|+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}Q^{2}\left(\chi^{\prime}_{B}-\frac{2}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\left(Q\partial_{y_{1}}Q\right)\left(\chi_{B}-\frac{2y_{1}}{B}\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{3,3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{48}}{B^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left|\nabla\eta\right|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

(4.44)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{49}}{B^{8}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left|\nabla\varepsilon\right|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B^{23}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y.

Here,
C_{48}>1
and
C_{49}>1
are some universal constants independent of
B
.

We see that (
4.41
) follows from (
4.42
), (
4.43
), (
4.44
) and
B
large enough.

Step 4.
Estimate on
\mathcal{G}_{4}
. We claim that, there exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<B^{-100}
small enough, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{50}}{B^{10}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

(4.45)

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{51}}{B^{10}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}){\rm d}y+C_{52}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{50}>1
and
C_{51}>1
are some universal constants independent of
B
and
C_{52}=C_{52}(B)
is a constant depending only on
B
.

Note that, from the definition of
{\rm{Mod}}_{\eta}
in (
4.31
), we have

\displaystyle\left|{\rm{Mod}_{\eta}}-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(b^{2}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)|\nabla\varepsilon|

\displaystyle+\left(b^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)\left(e^{-\frac{|y_{2}|}{3}}\mathbf{1}_{[-2,0]}(|b|^{\frac{3}{4}}y_{1})+e^{-\frac{|y|}{3}}\right)

\displaystyle+|b|(1+|y|)\left(b^{2}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left(e^{-\frac{|y_{2}|}{3}}\mathbf{1}_{[-2,0]}(|b|^{\frac{3}{4}}y_{1})+e^{-\frac{|y|}{3}}\right).

Based on the above estimate, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\rm{Mod}}_{\eta}-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon\right)\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(b^{4}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\left(b^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(e^{-\frac{2|y_{2}|}{3}}\mathbf{1}_{[-2,0]}(|b|^{\frac{3}{4}}y_{1})+e^{-\frac{2|y|}{3}}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+b^{2}\left(b^{4}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|}{10}}{\rm d}y\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1+|y|^{2})\left(e^{-\frac{2|y_{2}|}{3}}\mathbf{1}_{[-2,0]}(|b|^{\frac{3}{4}}y_{1})+e^{-\frac{2|y|}{3}}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y.

It follows from (
3.4
) and
\psi_{0,B}\lesssim\psi_{B}
that

\left\|\left({\rm{Mod}}_{\eta}-\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon\right)\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim B^{\frac{1}{2}}b^{2}+\frac{1}{B^{30}}\left\|\varepsilon\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{2}}.

Based on a similar argument and (ii) of Lemma
2.3
, we see that

\left\|\Psi_{b}\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Psi^{2}_{b}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim B^{\frac{1}{2}}b^{2}.

On the other hand, from the definitions of
R_{b}
and
R_{NL}
in Lemma
3.2
,

\left|R_{b}+R_{NL}\right|\lesssim|b||\varepsilon|+|b|\varepsilon^{2}+|\varepsilon|^{3}.

Therefore, from the 2D Sobolev embedding estimate, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(R_{b}+R_{NL}\right)\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{2}}
