\displaystyle\lesssim|b|\left\|\varepsilon\sqrt{\psi_{0,B}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\varepsilon\psi^{\frac{1}{6}}_{0,B}\right\|^{3}_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{30}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Here, we use the fact that

\psi_{0,B}^{\frac{1}{3}}\lesssim\psi_{B}\lesssim\varphi_{i,B}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\psi_{0,B}^{\frac{1}{3}}\lesssim B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}.

We see that (
4.45
) follows from the above estimates, Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.11
.

Step 5.
Conclusion. Combining the estimates (
4.39
),(
4.40
), (
4.41
) with (
4.45
), we complete the proof of Proposition
4.9
.

∎

4.3.
Energy-Virial Lyapunov functional

In this subsection, we introduce the energy-virial Lyapunov functional
\mathcal{M}_{i,j}
which will play a crucial role in closing the energy estimate in the bootstrap setting (
3.4
)–(
3.6
). We mention here that, the construction of this functional is based on the combination of the energy and virial quantity that we defined in §
4.1
and §
4.2
, respectively.

For
\left(i,j\right)\in\left\{1,2\right\}^{2}
, we define

\mathcal{M}_{i,j}=\mathcal{F}_{i,j}+\frac{1}{B^{20}}\mathcal{P}.

Proposition 4.12
.

There exist some universal constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<B^{-100}
small enough such that the following holds. Assume that for all
s\in[0,s_{0}]
, the solution
u(t)
with initial data
u_{0}
satisfies the bootstrap assumption (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) with
0<\kappa<\kappa_{1}
. Then for all
(i,j)\in\left\{1,2\right\}^{2}
and
s\in[0,s_{0}]
, the following estimates are true.

(i)

Coercivity.
It holds

\mathcal{N}_{i}\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{i,j}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{i}.

(ii)

Monotonicity formula
. There exists a universal constant
D>0
(independent of
B
), such that

\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}}{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}\right)+\frac{D}{B^{27}}\mathcal{N}_{i-1}\leq C_{53}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{53}=C_{53}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
.

Proof.

Proof of (i). On the one hand side, from (iii) of Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.7
,

\left|\mathcal{P}\right|\lesssim B^{9}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\eta^{2}\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\lesssim B^{16}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\lesssim B^{16}\mathcal{N}_{i}.

(4.46)

Here, we use the fact that
\psi_{0,B}\lesssim\min(\varphi_{i,B},\psi_{B})
and the definition of
\mathcal{N}_{i}
.

On the other hand side, we decompose

\mathcal{F}_{i,j}=\mathcal{N}_{i}-3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{i,B}{\rm d}y-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q_{b}\varepsilon^{3}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{4}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y.

First, by an elementary computation,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{i}-3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{L}(\varepsilon\sqrt{\psi_{B}}),\varepsilon\sqrt{\psi_{B}}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\left(\frac{(\psi^{\prime}_{B})^{2}}{\psi_{B}}-2\psi^{\prime\prime}_{B}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(\varphi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}){\rm d}y-3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(Q^{2}_{b}-Q^{2}\right)\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y.

Therefore, from (iv) of Proposition
2.1
, (i) of Lemma
3.4
and (
3.3
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{i}-3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q_{b}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\nu}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y

(4.47)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(\varphi_{i,B}-\psi_{B}){\rm d}y\geq\frac{1}{2}\min(1,\nu)\mathcal{N}_{i}.

Second, from (
3.4
), the definition of
\mathcal{J}_{i,j}
in (
4.1
) and (iii) of Lemma
3.6
,

\left|\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\right|\lesssim\left|J_{1}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{10}}\Longrightarrow\left|\mathcal{J}_{i,j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{i,B}{\rm d}y\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{10}}\mathcal{N}_{i}.

(4.48)

Next, using again the 2D Sobolev embedding and (i) of Lemma
3.4
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q_{b}\varepsilon^{3}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\varepsilon\sqrt{\psi_{B}}\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}

(4.49)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{10}}\mathcal{N}_{i}.

Last, from (
4.6
), we obtain

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{4}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y\right|\lesssim\|\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{B^{10}}\mathcal{N}_{i}.

(4.50)

Combining (
4.46
), (
4.47
), (
4.48
), (
4.49
) with (
4.50
), we complete the proof of (i).

Proof of (ii). From Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
4.9
, we see that

\displaystyle\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}}{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y+\frac{\nu_{1}}{B^{21}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\eta|^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{54}}{B^{5}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y+\frac{C_{55}}{B^{28}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+C_{56}b^{4}.

Here,
C_{54}>1
and
C_{55}>1
are some universal constants independent of
B
and
C_{56}=C_{56}(B)>1
is a constant dependent only on
B
. Therefore, from Lemma
4.7
, there exists a universal constant
\nu_{4}>0
(independent of
B
) such that

\displaystyle\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}}{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}\right)+\frac{1}{5}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu_{4}}{B^{27}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{0,B}{\rm d}y\leq\frac{C_{55}}{B^{28}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+C_{56}b^{4}.

Based on the above estimate and
\psi_{B}\lesssim B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}
on
\mathbb{R}
, we deduce that

\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{\mathcal{M}_{i,j}}{\lambda^{\theta(j-1)}}\right)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\left(\frac{1}{6}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\frac{\nu_{4}}{2B^{27}}\psi_{0,B}\right){\rm d}y\leq C_{56}b^{4}.

It follows from
\psi_{B}\lesssim B\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\psi_{0,B}
and (v) of Lemma
3.4
that

\frac{\psi_{B}}{B^{27}}+\frac{\varphi_{i-1,B}}{B^{27}}\lesssim\frac{\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}}{B^{17}}+\frac{\psi_{B}}{B^{27}}\lesssim\frac{1}{6}\varphi^{\prime}_{i,B}+\frac{\nu_{4}}{2B^{27}}\psi_{0,B}.

Combining the above two estimates, we complete the proof of (ii).
∎

4.4.
Decay property on the
y_{1}
-variable

In this subsection, we will introduce an elementary estimate of the decay for the remainder term
\varepsilon
on the right-hand side of
y_{1}
-variable. We mention here that, this estimate will be used to close the bootstrap estimate (
3.6
) at the end of proof of Theorem
1.2
in §
5
.

We define the smooth function
\Phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
as follows,

\Phi(y_{1})=\begin{cases}y_{1}^{100},&\text{ for }y_{1}>1,\\
0,&\text{ for }y_{1}<0,\end{cases}\quad\mbox{with}\quad\Phi^{\prime}\geq 0,\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}.

Proposition 4.13
.

Under the assumption of Proposition
4.12
, we have

\frac{1}{\lambda^{100}}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\lambda^{100}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\Phi{\rm d}y\right)\lesssim b^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y.

Proof.

Using (
3.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\Phi{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon\Phi\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon+\partial_{y_{1}}\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon\right)\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon\Phi\left({\rm{Mod}}-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\Lambda\varepsilon+\Psi_{b}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon\Phi\partial_{y_{1}}\left(\left(Q_{b}+\varepsilon\right)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b}\right){\rm d}y.

From integration by parts and the fact that
y_{1}\Phi^{\prime}=100\Phi
for
y_{1}\geq 1
and
\Phi^{\prime\prime\prime}\ll\Phi^{\prime}
for
y_{1}
large, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon\Phi\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\Lambda\varepsilon+\partial_{y_{1}}\left(-\Delta\varepsilon+\varepsilon\right)\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}y_{1}\Phi^{\prime}{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(3(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\Phi^{\prime}{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\Phi^{\prime\prime\prime}{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=-50\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\Phi{\rm d}y-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(3(\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}+(\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon)^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\Phi^{\prime}{\rm d}y+O\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y\right).

Recall that, from the definition of
{\rm{Mod}}
in §
3.1
,

\displaystyle\left({\rm{Mod}}-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\Lambda\varepsilon+\Psi_{b}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\Lambda Q_{b}+\left(\frac{x_{1s}}{\lambda}-1\right)\left(\partial_{y_{1}}Q_{b}+\partial_{y_{1}}\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{x_{2s}}{\lambda}\left(\partial_{y_{2}}Q_{b}+\partial_{y_{2}}\varepsilon\right)+\Psi_{b}-b_{s}\frac{\partial Q_{b}}{\partial b}.

Therefore, from (i) and (ii) of Lemma
2.3
and (ii) of Lemma
3.6
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon\Phi\left({\rm{Mod}}-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b\right)\Lambda\varepsilon+\Psi_{b}\right){\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{B^{10}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\Phi^{\prime}{\rm d}y.

Using integration by parts, the non-linear term can be rewritten by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon\Phi\partial_{y_{1}}\left(\left(Q_{b}+\varepsilon\right)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}Q_{b}\left(2\Phi\partial_{y_{1}}Q_{b}-\Phi^{\prime}Q_{b}\right){\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{3}\left(\Phi\partial_{y_{1}}Q_{b}-\Phi^{\prime}Q_{b}\right){\rm d}y-\frac{3}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{4}\Phi^{\prime}{\rm d}y.

Note that

\left\|\frac{\nabla\left(\Phi^{\prime}+\psi_{B}\right)}{\Phi^{\prime}+\psi_{B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\sum_{|\alpha|=2}\left\|\frac{\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\left(\Phi^{\prime}+\psi_{B}\right)}{\Phi^{\prime}+\psi_{B}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim 1.

Therefore, using again (
4.6
) and (i) of Lemma
2.3
, we obtain

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon\Phi\partial_{y_{1}}\left(\left(Q_{b}+\varepsilon\right)^{3}-Q^{3}_{b}\right){\rm d}y\right|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y+\frac{1}{B^{10}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\Phi^{\prime}{\rm d}y.

Combining the above estimates, for
B
large enough, we obtain

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\Phi{\rm d}y+100\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\Phi{\rm d}y\lesssim b^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}\psi_{B}{\rm d}y,

which completes the proof of Proposition
4.13
immediately.
∎

5.
End of the proof of Theorem
1.2

Let
0<\alpha\ll\alpha^{*}\ll\kappa\ll 1
to be chosen later. Recall that, in Definition
1.1
, we define a
L^{2}
-moduled tube
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
and a set of initial data
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
.
In this section, we will classify the asymptotic behavior of any solution with initial data in
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
which directly implies Theorem
1.2
.
We start with the following definition,

t^{*}=\sup\{0<t<+\infty:u(t_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}},\ \forall t_{1}\in[0,t]\}.

(5.1)

Since
0<\alpha\ll\alpha^{*}\ll\kappa\ll 1
, then for any initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
, we have
t^{*}>0
.

Next, by Proposition
3.1
, we know that
u(t)
admits the following geometrical decomposition on
[0,t^{*}]
:

u(t,x)=\frac{1}{{\lambda}(t)}\left[{Q}_{b(t)}+{\varepsilon}(t)\right]\left(\frac{x-{x}(t)}{{\lambda}(t)}\right).

Using the fact that
u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon(0)\|_{H^{1}}+|b(0)|+

\displaystyle|1-\lambda(0)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(0)\lesssim\delta(\alpha),

(5.2)

\displaystyle|E(u_{0})|+\bigg|\int u_{0}^{2}-\int Q^{2}\bigg|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)

\displaystyle\mbox{and}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}y_{1}^{100}\varepsilon^{2}(0,y){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\leq 2.

We fix constants
B>100
large enough and
0<\kappa_{1}<\min\left\{\kappa_{*},B^{-100}\right\}
small enough such that Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
4.9
hold. Define

t^{**}=\sup\{0<t<t^{*}:\eqref{est:Boot1}-\eqref{est:Boot3}\ \mbox{hold for all }t_{1}\in[0,t]\}.

(5.3)

Note that from (
5.2
) and a straightforward continuity argument, we have
t^{**}>0
is well-defined. The key point in our analysis is to deduce
t^{*}=t^{**}
by improving the bootstrap assumptions (
3.4
)–(
3.6
).
From now on, we denote
s^{*}=s(t^{*})
,
s^{**}=s(t^{**})
.
In the remainder of the proof, the implied constants in
\lesssim
and
O
do not depend on the small constant
\kappa
appearing in bootstrap assumptions (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) but can depend on the large constant
B
.

5.1.
Consequence of the monotonicity formula

We derive some crucial estimates from the monotonicity formula introduced in Proposition
4.12
. The proof of the following Lemma is similar to
[
35
, Lemma 4.3]
, but it is given for the sake of completeness and the readers’ convenience.

Lemma 5.1
.

The following estimates hold.

(i)

Control of
b
. For all
0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}
and
m=2,3,4
, we have

\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}|b(s)|^{m}\,{\rm d}s\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1})+|b(s_{1})|^{m-1}+|b(s_{2})|^{m-1}.

(ii)

Control of
\mathcal{N}_{i}
.
For all
0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}
and
i=1,2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{i}(s_{2})+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i-1}(s){\rm d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{i}(s_{1})+|b(s_{1})|^{3}+|b(s_{2})|^{3},

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{N}_{i}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{2})}+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}\left(\mathcal{N}_{i-1}(s)+|b(s)|^{3}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{i}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{2})}.

(iii)

Control of
\frac{b}{\lambda^{\theta}}
. For all
0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}
, we have

\left|\frac{b(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{2})}-\frac{b(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}\right|\leq K\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{2})}\right).

Here,
K>1
is a universal constant.

(iv)

Refined control of
\lambda
. Let
\lambda_{0}(s)=\lambda(s)(1-J_{1}(s))^{2}
. Then for all
s\in[0,s^{**}]
,

\bigg|\frac{\lambda_{0s}}{\lambda_{0}}+b\bigg|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{0}+b^{2}.

Proof.

Proof of (i)–(ii).
From (
3.9
), we have

b^{2}=-\frac{b_{s}}{\theta}+O\left(\mathcal{N}_{0}+|b|\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+|b|^{3}\right)\Longrightarrow\frac{3}{4}b^{2}+\frac{b_{s}}{\theta}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(5.4)

Note that, for
m=2,3,4
, we also have

b_{s}|b|^{m-2}=\frac{1}{m-1}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(b|b|^{m-2}\right).

Based on the above identities, (
3.4
) and (
5.4
), for all
0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}
, we have

\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}|b(s)|^{m}{\rm d}s\lesssim\kappa^{m-2}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\mathcal{N}_{0}(s){\rm d}s+|b(s_{1})|^{m-1}+|b(s_{2})|^{m-1}.

Next, from (i) and (ii) of Proposition
4.12
, for
i=1,2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{i}(s_{2})+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\mathcal{N}_{i-1}(s)\,{\rm d}s\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{i}(s_{1})+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}|b(s)|^{4}\,{\rm d}s.

The above two estimates imply (i) and the first estimate in (ii) immediately.

Then, using again (
5.4
), we have

\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{|b(s)|^{3}}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}{\rm d}s+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b_{s}(s)|b(s)|}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}{\rm d}s\lesssim\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}{\rm d}s,

which implies

\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{|b(s)|^{3}}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}{\rm d}s\lesssim\frac{b^{2}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{2})}+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}{\rm d}s.

Using again (i) and (ii) of Proposition
4.12
, we deduce that

\frac{\mathcal{N}_{i}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{2})}+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{i-1}(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}{\rm d}s\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{i}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}+\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{b^{4}(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}.

The above two estimates imply the second estimate in (ii) immediately.

Proof of (iii). First, from (iii) of Lemma
3.6
, we have

|e^{J}-1|\lesssim|J|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\delta(\kappa)\ll 1.

Based on (iii) of Lemma
3.7
and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
, for

0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{b(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{2})}e^{J(s_{2})}-\frac{b(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}e^{J(s_{1})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\left|\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{b}{\lambda^{\theta}}e^{J}\right)(s){\rm d}s\right|\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s_{2})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s_{2})}.

On the other hand, we have

\left|\frac{b}{\lambda^{\theta}}e^{J}-\frac{b}{\lambda^{\theta}}\right|\lesssim\frac{|b|\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lambda^{\theta}}\lesssim\frac{b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{1}}{\lambda^{\theta}}.

Combining the above two estimates, we complete the proof of (iii).

Proof of (iv). By an elementary computation,

\left|\frac{\lambda_{0s}}{\lambda_{0}}+b-\left(\frac{\lambda_{s}}{\lambda}+b-2J_{1s}\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{|J_{1}||J_{1s}|}{1-J_{1}}.

Note that, from (ii) and (iii) of Lemma
3.6
and (i) of Lemma
3.7
, we have

\left|J_{1}\right|+\left|J_{1s}\right|\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining the above estimates with Lemma
3.7
, we complete the proof of (iv).
∎

5.2.
Rigidity dynamics in
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}

In this subsection, we will give a specific classification for the asymptotic behavior of solutions with initial data in
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
. We denote

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}(t)=\mathcal{N}_{1}(t)+b^{2}(t),\quad\mbox{on}\ [0,t^{*}].

Denote
t_{1}^{*}
by the following separation time,

\displaystyle t_{1}^{*}=\begin{cases}0,&\mbox{if}\ |b(0)|\geq C^{*}\widetilde{N}_{1}(0),\\
\sup\left\{0<t<t^{*}:|b(t_{1})|\leq C^{*}\widetilde{N}_{1}(t_{1}),\forall t_{1}\in[0,t]\right\},\quad&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Here
C^{*}=100K
and
K
is the constant introduced in Lemma
5.1
.
Then the following rigidity dynamics of solution flows near soliton manifold hold.

Proposition 5.2

(Rigidity Dynamics)
.

There exist universal constants
0<\alpha\ll\alpha^{*}\ll\kappa
such that the following holds. Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
and
u(t)
be the corresponding solution to (
1.1
) on
[0,T)
. Then the following trichotomy holds:

Soliton:

If
t^{*}=t_{1}^{*}
, then
t^{*}=t_{1}^{*}=T=\infty
with

\displaystyle\lambda(t)=\lambda_{\infty}\left(1+o(1)\right),\quad|b(t)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(t)\rightarrow 0,\ \mbox{as}\ t\to\infty,

(5.5)

\displaystyle x_{1}(t)=\frac{t}{\lambda_{\infty}^{2}}\left(1+o(1)\right),\quad\quad x_{2}(t)\rightarrow x_{2,\infty},\ \ \mbox{as}\ t\to\infty,

for some
(\lambda_{\infty},x_{2,\infty})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. In addition, we have
|\lambda_{\infty}-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha_{0})
.

Exit:

If
t^{*}>t_{1}^{*}
with
b(t^{*}_{1})\leq-C^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}(t^{*}_{1})
, then
t^{*}<T
. In particular,

\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{0}>0\\
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\end{subarray}}\left\|u(t^{*})-\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}}Q\left(\frac{x-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}=\alpha^{*}.

(5.6)

Moreover, the following estimates hold

b(t^{*})\leq-C(\alpha^{*})<0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lambda(t^{*})\geq\frac{C(\alpha^{*})}{\delta(\alpha)}\gg 1.

(5.7)

Blow-up:

If
t^{*}>t_{1}^{*}
with
b(t^{*}_{1})\geq C^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}(t_{1}^{*})
, then
t^{*}=T<\infty
. In particular

\displaystyle\lim_{t\uparrow T}\|\nabla\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}}=0,\quad\lim_{t\uparrow T}\frac{\lambda(t)}{(T-t)^{\frac{1}{3-\theta}}}=\ell_{1}(u_{0}),

(5.8)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\uparrow T}\frac{b(t)}{(T-t)^{\frac{\theta}{3-\theta}}}=\ell_{2}(u_{0}),\quad x_{1}(t)\sim(T-t)^{-\frac{\theta-1}{3-\theta}}.

Here,
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
are positive constants depending only on the initial data
u_{0}
.

Note that, Proposition
5.2
classifies the behavior of solution flows near the soliton manifold which implies Theorem
1.2
. In the rest of this section, we are devoted to the proof of Proposition
5.2
and split the proof into the following three parts.

5.2.1.
The Soliton case.

Assume that
t^{*}=t_{1}^{*}
,
i.e.
for all
t\in[0,t^{*}]
,

|b(t)|\leq C^{*}\big[\mathcal{N}_{1}(t)+b^{2}(t)\big]\Longrightarrow|b(t)|\leq 2C^{*}\mathcal{N}_{1}(t).

(5.9)

Step 1. Closing the bootstrap. We claim that, for all
s\in[0,s^{**}]
,

\displaystyle|b(s)|+\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)+\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1}){\rm d}s_{1}\lesssim\delta(\alpha),

(5.10)

\displaystyle|\lambda(s)-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}y_{1}^{100}\varepsilon^{2}(s,y){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\leq 5.

Note that, from
0<\alpha\ll\alpha^{*}\ll\kappa
, the estimates in (
5.10
) strictly improve the boostrap estimates (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) and so we obtain
t^{*}=t^{**}
.

Indeed, from (
5.4
), (
5.9
) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
, for all
s\in[0,s^{**}]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|b(s)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|b(0)\right|+\int_{0}^{s}\left(b^{2}(s_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{0}(s_{1})\right){\rm d}s_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|b(0)\right|+\int_{0}^{s}\left(4\left(C^{*}\right)^{2}\mathcal{N}_{1}^{2}(s_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{0}(s_{1})\right){\rm d}s_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim|b(0)|+|b(0)|^{3}+\mathcal{N}_{2}(0)+|b(s)|^{3}.

It follows from (
5.2
), (i) of Lemma
3.6
and (i) of Lemma
5.1
that

\left|b(s)\right|+\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)+\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1}){\rm d}s_{1}\lesssim\delta(\alpha),\quad\mbox{for all}\ s\in[0,s^{**}].

(5.11)

Then we use (
5.9
) and (iv) of Lemma
5.1
to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\lambda_{0s}}{\lambda_{0}}\right|\lesssim b+\mathcal{N}_{0}+b^{2}\lesssim 4C^{*}\mathcal{N}_{1}+\mathcal{N}_{0}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}.

Integrating the above estimate over
[s_{1},s_{2}]
for any
0\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}
and then using (
5.2
) and (
5.11
), we deduce that

\left|\frac{\lambda_{0}(s_{2})}{\lambda_{0}(s_{1})}-1\right|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)\Longrightarrow|\lambda(s)-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)\ \mbox{on}\ [0,s^{**}].

(5.12)

Last, integrating the estimate in Proposition
4.13
over
[0,s]
for any
s\in[0,s^{**}]
and then using (
5.2
), (
5.12
) and (i) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(s)\Phi{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\lambda^{100}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{100}(s)}\left(b^{2}(s_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1})\right){\rm d}s_{1}

(5.13)

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda^{100}(0)}{\lambda^{100}(s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(0)\Phi{\rm d}y\leq 2+\delta(\alpha)\leq 5.

Here,
C>1
is a universal constant independent with
\alpha
. Combining (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) with (
5.13
), we complete the proof of (
5.10
), and thus, from a standard continuity argument, we obtain
t^{*}=t^{**}=T=\infty
.

Step 2. Proof of (
5.5
).
From (
5.10
) and (i) of Lemma
5.1
, we know that
\mathcal{N}_{1}\in L^{1}((0,+\infty))
, and thus, there exists an increasing sequence
\{s_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset(0,+\infty)
such that
s_{n}\rightarrow+\infty
and
\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{n})\rightarrow 0
as
n\to\infty
. Then, from (
5.9
) and (i) of Lemma
5.1
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
s\geq s_{n}
, we have

\mathcal{N}_{1}(s)\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{n})+\mathcal{N}_{1}^{3}(s)+\mathcal{N}_{1}^{3}(s_{n})\Longrightarrow\lim_{s\to\infty}\mathcal{N}_{1}(s)=0.

From (
4.9
), (
5.9
) and (
5.10
), we deduce that

\mathcal{N}_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{N}^{\frac{92}{93}}_{1}\ \mbox{and}\ |b|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{1}\Longrightarrow\lim_{s\to\infty}\left(\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)+|b(s)|\right)=0.

Next, from (
5.9
), (
5.10
) and (i) of Lemma
5.1
, we see that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{0}(s)\right)\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{N}_{0}(s)+\mathcal{N}^{2}_{1}(s)\right){\rm d}s_{1}\lesssim\delta(\alpha).

It follows from (
5.10
) and (iv) of Lemma
5.1
that

\int_{0}^{\infty}|\lambda_{0s}(s)|{\rm d}s\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{0}(s)\right){\rm d}s\lesssim\delta(\alpha).

Based on above estimate, (
5.2
) and (
5.10
), we know that there exists
\lambda_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
with
|\lambda_{\infty}-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)
such that

\lim_{s\to\infty}\lambda_{0}(s)=\lambda_{\infty}\Longrightarrow\lim_{s\to\infty}\lambda(s)=\lambda_{\infty}.

Then, from the above estimates, (ii) of Lemma
3.6
and
t\sim s
,

x_{1t}=\frac{x_{1s}}{\lambda^{3}}=\frac{1+o(1)}{\lambda^{2}_{\infty}}\Longrightarrow x_{1}(t)=\frac{t}{\lambda^{2}_{\infty}}\left(1+o(1)\right).

Last, from (
5.10
), (ii) of Lemma
3.6
and (iv) of Lemma
3.7
, we have

\left|x_{2s}-\left(\lambda J_{3}\right)_{s}\right|\lesssim|b|\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}+b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{0}\lesssim b^{2}+\mathcal{N}_{0}.

It follows from (i) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|x_{2s}-\left(\lambda J_{3}\right)_{s}\right|{\rm d}s\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(b^{2}(s)+\mathcal{N}_{0}(s)\right){\rm d}s\lesssim\delta(\alpha).

Based on the above estimate and
|\lambda(s)J_{3}(s)|\lesssim|J_{3}(s)|\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\to 0
as
s\to\infty
, we know that there exists
x_{2,\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\lim_{s\to\infty}\left(x_{2}(s)-\lambda(s)J_{3}(s)\right)=x_{2,\infty}\Longrightarrow\lim_{s\to\infty}x_{2}(s)=x_{2,\infty}.

Combining the above estimates with
t\sim s
, we complete the proof of (
5.5
).

5.2.2.
The Exit case.

Assume that
t^{*}>t_{1}^{*}
and
b(t^{*}_{1})\leq-C^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}(t_{1}^{*})
.

Step 1. Closing the bootstrap.
First of all, using the same argument as in the Soliton case, the following estimates hold on
[0,s_{1}^{*}]
:

\displaystyle|b(s)|+\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)+\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1}){\rm d}s_{1}\lesssim\delta(\alpha),

(5.14)

\displaystyle|\lambda(s)-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}y_{1}^{100}\varepsilon^{2}(s,y){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\leq 5.

In particular, we have
t_{1}^{*}<t^{**}\leq t^{*}
. Now, we claim that
t^{*}=t^{**}<T
. To prove this, we use a slightly different bootstrap argument than the one used in the Soliton case to improve the bootstrap assumptions (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) on
[t_{1}^{*},t^{**}]
.

We denote

\ell^{*}=\frac{b(s^{*}_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s^{*}_{1})}<0.

From (
5.14
), we deduce that
|\ell^{*}|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)
. From (
5.14
), (iii) of Lemma
5.1
,
|b(s^{*}_{1})|\geq C^{*}|{N}_{1}(s_{1}^{*})|
,
and the definition of
C^{*}
, for all
s\in[s_{1}^{*},s^{**}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}-\ell^{*}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}_{1}(s^{*}_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s^{*}_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s^{*}_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s^{*}_{1})}+\frac{b^{2}(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\ell^{*}|}{100}+\delta(\alpha)|\ell^{*}|+\kappa\frac{|b(s)|}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)},

which implies immediately

2\ell^{*}\leq\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}\leq\frac{\ell^{*}}{2}<0\quad\mbox{and}\quad b(s)<0.

(5.15)

It follows from (
5.14
) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
that

\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}\lesssim\delta(\alpha).

On the other hand, from (iii) of Lemma
5.1
and
b(s)<0
on
[s^{*}_{1},s^{**}]
, we obtain

\frac{\lambda_{0s}}{\lambda_{0}}\gtrsim-\mathcal{N}_{0}\gtrsim-\kappa\Longrightarrow\frac{\lambda_{0}(s_{2})}{\lambda_{0}(s_{1})}-1\gtrsim-\kappa,\quad\mbox{for all}\ s_{1}^{*}\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}.

Therefore, from
|J_{1}|\lesssim\kappa\ll 1
on
[s^{*}_{1},s^{**}]
, we directly have

\frac{\lambda(s_{2})}{\lambda(s_{1})}-1\gtrsim-\kappa,\quad\mbox{for all}\ s_{1}^{*}\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}\Longrightarrow\lambda(s)\geq\frac{1}{2}\ \mbox{on}\ [s^{*}_{1},s^{**}].

(5.16)

Then, using a similar argument to the one in the soliton case, for all
s\in[s_{1}^{*},s^{**}]
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(s)\Phi{\rm d}y\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}y_{1}^{100}\varepsilon^{2}(s^{*}_{1},y){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}+\delta(\kappa)\leq 7.

Last, for all
t\in[t^{*}_{1},t^{*})
, we have
u(t)\in\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
. Therefore, from Proposition
3.1
,

|b(t)|\lesssim\delta(\alpha^{*})\ll\kappa,\quad\mbox{on}\ [t_{1}^{*},t^{*}].

It follows from (
5.2
), (i) of Lemma
3.6
and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
that

\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}+|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)\lesssim\delta(\alpha)+\delta(\alpha^{*})\ll\kappa,\quad\mbox{on}\ [s_{1}^{*},s^{**}].

Combining the above estimates, we improve the bootstrap assumptions (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) on
[s_{1}^{*},s^{**}]
and thus we conclude that
t^{*}=t^{**}
.

Step 2. Proof of (
5.6
) and (
5.7
). First, from (
5.15
) and (iv) of Lemma
5.1
, we have

\frac{|\ell^{*}|}{3}-C\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{\lambda^{\theta}}\leq\lambda_{0}^{2-\theta}\lambda_{0t}\leq 3|\ell^{*}|+C\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{\lambda^{\theta}}.

Here,
C
is a universal constant independent with
\alpha
and
\alpha^{*}
. Integrating the above estimate over
[t_{1}^{*},t]
for any
t\in[t_{1}^{*},t^{*}]
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{|\ell^{*}|}{3}(t-t_{1}^{*})-C\int_{t_{1}^{*}}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}(t_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(t_{1})}{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t)-\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t^{*}_{1})}{3-\theta},

\displaystyle 3|\ell^{*}|(t-t_{1}^{*})+C\int_{t_{1}^{*}}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}(t_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(t_{1})}{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t)-\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t^{*}_{1})}{3-\theta}.

Note that, from (
3.4
), (
5.16
) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}^{*}}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}(t_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(t_{1})}{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s^{*}_{1}}^{s}\lambda^{3-\theta}(s_{1})\mathcal{N}_{0}(s_{1}){\rm d}s_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{3-\theta}(s)\int_{s_{1}^{*}}^{s}\mathcal{N}_{0}(s_{1}){\rm d}s_{1}\lesssim\delta(\kappa)\lambda^{3-\theta}(t).

Combining the above two estimates with
\lambda_{0}\approx\lambda
, we obtain

\frac{1}{5}\left(|\ell^{*}|(t-t_{1}^{*})+\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t^{*}_{1})\right)\leq\lambda^{3-\theta}(t)\leq 5\left(|\ell^{*}|(t-t_{1}^{*})+\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t^{*}_{1})\right).

Based on the above estimate and (
5.15
),

\displaystyle-10|\ell^{*}|\left(|\ell^{*}|(t-t_{1}^{*})+\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t^{*}_{1})\right)^{\frac{\theta}{3-\theta}}

\displaystyle\leq b(t),

(5.17)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{10}|\ell^{*}|\left(|\ell^{*}|(t-t_{1}^{*})+\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t^{*}_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\geq b(t).

The above lower and upper estimate of
b(t)
is enough to show that
t^{*}<T
.

Indeed, for the sake of contradiction assume that
t^{*}=T
. Then from (
3.4
), (
5.2
) and (
5.16
), we obtain

\left\|\nabla\varepsilon(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\delta(\kappa)\Longrightarrow\left\|\nabla u(t)\right\|_{L^{2}}=\frac{\|Q\|_{L^{2}}+\delta(\kappa)}{\lambda(t)}\lesssim 1.

It follows from the blow-up criterion (
1.2
) that
T=\infty
. Therefore, from (
5.17
), we obtain
b(t)\to-\infty
as
t\to\infty
which contradictory with
|b(t)|\lesssim\delta(\kappa)
. This means that
t^{*}<T
and thus (
5.6
) holds at time
t^{*}
from the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
.

Last, from (
5.2
) and (i) of Lemma
3.6
, we have

\left(\alpha^{*}\right)^{2}\lesssim|b(t^{*})|
, and thus from (
5.15
) and
|\ell^{*}|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)
, we complete the proof of (
5.7
).

5.2.3.
The Blow-up case.

Assume that
t^{*}>t_{1}^{*}
and

b(t_{1}^{*})\geq C^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}(t_{1}^{*})
.

Step 1. Closing the bootstrap. First of all, using the same argument as in the Soliton case, the following estimates hold on
[0,s_{1}^{*}]
:

\displaystyle|b(s)|+\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)+\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1}){\rm d}s_{1}\lesssim\delta(\alpha),

(5.18)

\displaystyle|\lambda(s)-1|\lesssim\delta(\alpha)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{0}^{\infty}y_{1}^{100}\varepsilon^{2}(s,y){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}\leq 5.

In particular, we have
t_{1}^{*}<t^{**}\leq t^{*}
. Now, we claim that
t^{*}=t^{**}=T
. To prove this, we use a slightly different bootstrap argument than the one used in the Soliton and Exit cases to improve the bootstrap assumptions (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) on
[t_{1}^{*},t^{**}]
.

We denote

\ell^{*}=\frac{b(s^{*}_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(s^{*}_{1})}>0.

From (
5.18
), we deduce that
0<\ell^{*}\lesssim\delta(\alpha)
. Based on (
5.18
), (iii) of Lemma
5.1
and a similar argument to the one in the Exit case, we obtain

\frac{1}{2}\ell^{*}\leq\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}\leq 2\ell^{*}\quad\mbox{and}\quad b(s)>0.

(5.19)

It follows from (
5.18
) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
that

\frac{b(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}\lesssim\delta(\alpha).

(5.20)

On the other hand, from (iii) of Lemma
5.1
and
b(s)>0
on
[s^{*}_{1},s^{**}]
, we obtain

\frac{\lambda_{0s}}{\lambda_{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{0}\lesssim\kappa\Longrightarrow\frac{\lambda_{0}(s_{2})}{\lambda_{0}(s_{1})}-1\lesssim\kappa,\quad\mbox{for all}\ s_{1}^{*}\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}.

Therefore, from
|J_{1}|\lesssim\kappa\ll 1
on
[s^{*}_{1},s^{**}]
, we directly have

\frac{\lambda(s_{2})}{\lambda(s_{1})}-1\lesssim\kappa,\quad\mbox{for all}\ s_{1}^{*}\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq s^{**}\Longrightarrow\lambda(s)\leq 2\ \mbox{on}\ [s^{*}_{1},s^{**}].

(5.21)

Therefore, from (
5.20
) and (i) of Lemma
3.6
, we deduce that

\|\varepsilon(s)\|_{L^{2}}+|b(s)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(s)\lesssim\delta(\alpha).

(5.22)

Last, integrating the estimate in Proposition
4.13
over
[s^{*}_{1},s]
for any
s\in[s^{*}_{1},s^{**}]
and then using (
5.18
) and (i) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(s)\Phi{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{s_{1}^{*}}^{s}\frac{\lambda^{100}(s_{1})}{\lambda^{100}(s)}\left(b^{2}(s_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{1}(s_{1})\right){\rm d}s_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda^{100}(s_{1}^{*})}{\lambda^{100}(s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varepsilon^{2}(s_{1}^{*})\Phi{\rm d}y\leq 2+\delta(\alpha)\leq\frac{5+\delta(\alpha)}{\lambda^{100}(s)}.

Combining the above estimate, we improve the bootstrap estimates (
3.4
)–(
3.6
) and thus we conclude that
t^{*}=t^{**}
. In particular, we also conclude that
t^{*}=t^{**}=T
since (
5.22
) improve the estimate in the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\alpha^{*}}
provided that
0<\alpha\ll\alpha^{*}
.

Step 2. Proof of (
5.8
). Similar to the case of Exit, for all
t\in[t^{*}_{1},T)
, we have

-3\ell^{*}-C\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{\lambda^{\theta}}\leq\lambda_{0}^{2-\theta}\lambda_{0t}\leq-\frac{1}{3}\ell^{*}+C\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{\lambda^{\theta}}.

Here,
C
is a universal constant independent with
\alpha
and
\alpha^{*}
. Integrating the above estimate over
[t_{1}^{*},t]
for any
t\in[t_{1}^{*},t^{*}]
, we deduce that

\displaystyle-3\ell^{*}(t-t^{*}_{1})-C\int_{t^{*}_{1}}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}(t_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(t_{1})}{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda^{3-\theta}_{0}(t)-\lambda^{3-\theta}_{0}(t_{1}^{*})}{3-\theta},

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\ell^{*}(t-t^{*}_{1})+C\int_{t^{*}_{1}}^{t}\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}(t_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(t_{1})}{\rm d}t_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lambda^{3-\theta}_{0}(t)-\lambda^{3-\theta}_{0}(t_{1}^{*})}{3-\theta}.

From (
5.21
) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
, we know that

\int_{t_{1}^{*}}^{t}\frac{N_{0}(t_{1})}{\lambda^{\theta}(t_{1})}{\rm d}t_{1}\lesssim\int_{s_{1}^{*}}^{s}\lambda^{3-\theta}(s_{1})\mathcal{N}_{0}(s_{1}){\rm d}s_{1}\lesssim\delta(\kappa).

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

0\leq\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t)\leq\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t_{1}^{*})-\left(1-\frac{\theta}{3}\right)\ell^{*}(t-t_{1}^{*})+\delta(\kappa),

which directly implies
T<\infty
.

Then, from (
5.2
), (
5.21
), (
5.22
) and (i) of Lemma
3.6
, we find

\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\lambda^{2}E(u_{0})+\delta(\alpha)\lesssim\delta(\alpha).

It follows that

\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\frac{\|\nabla Q\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\delta(\alpha)}{\lambda^{2}(t)},\quad\mbox{for all}\ t\in[0,T).

Based on the above identity, blow-up criterion (
1.2
) of the Cauchy-problem and (
5.20
),

\lim_{t\uparrow T}\lambda(t)=0\Longrightarrow\lim_{t\uparrow T}\left(\lambda(t)+|b(t)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(t)\right)=0.

Using again (i) of Lemma
3.6
, we conclude that

\lim_{t\uparrow T}\left(\lambda(t)+|b(t)|+\mathcal{N}_{2}(t)+\|\nabla\varepsilon\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)=0.

Then, from (
5.20
), (iii) of Lemma
3.7
and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left|\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}s}\left(\frac{b}{\lambda^{\theta}}e^{J}\right)\right|{\rm d}s\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{|b(s)|^{3}+\mathcal{N}_{0}(s)}{\lambda^{\theta}(s)}{\rm d}s<\infty.

On the other hand, based on the above estimates, we directly obtain

\lim_{t\uparrow T}\left(\left|J(t)\right|+\left|\frac{\lambda_{0}(t)}{\lambda(t)}-1\right|\right)=0.

Hence, there exists
0<\ell_{0}\lesssim\delta(\alpha)
such that

\lim_{t\uparrow T}\frac{b(t)}{\lambda^{\theta}(t)}=\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\frac{b(t)}{\lambda_{0}^{\theta}(t)}=\ell_{0}.

It follows from (ii) and (iv) of Lemma
5.1
that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(t)}{3-\theta}-\int_{t}^{T}\frac{b(t_{1})}{\lambda_{0}^{\theta}(t_{1})}{\rm d}t_{1}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{s}^{s(T)}\lambda_{0}^{3-\theta}(s_{1})\left(\frac{\lambda_{0s}(s_{1})}{\lambda_{0}(s_{1})}+b(s_{1})\right){\rm d}s_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{3-\theta}(s(t))\int_{s(t)}^{s(T)}\left(b^{2}(s_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{0}(s_{1})\right){\rm d}s_{1}\to 0\quad\mbox{as}\ t\to T.

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\uparrow T}\frac{\lambda(t)}{(T-t)^{\frac{1}{3-\theta}}}

\displaystyle=\left((3-\theta)\ell_{0}\right)^{\frac{1}{3-\theta}},

\displaystyle\lim_{t\uparrow T}\frac{b(t)}{(T-t)^{\frac{\theta}{3-\theta}}}

\displaystyle=\left((3-\theta)^{\theta}\ell_{0}^{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{3-\theta}}.

Last, from (ii) of Lemma
3.6
, we obtain
x_{1t}\sim\lambda^{-2}
which implies

x_{1}(t)\sim(T-t)^{-\frac{\theta-1}{3-\theta}},\quad\text{as }t\rightarrow T.

The proof of Proposition
5.2
is complete.

6.
End of the proof of Theorem
1.3

In this section, we will give a complete proof of Theorem
1.3
. First, we recall the following variational property of the ground state
Q
.

Lemma 6.1

(Variation property of
Q
)
.

There exists
\alpha_{1}>0
such that the following hold. For any
0<\alpha_{2}<\alpha_{1}
and
u_{0}\in H^{1}
with

E(u_{0})\leq\alpha_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u_{0}|^{2}{\rm d}y\quad\mbox{and}\quad\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(u_{0}^{2}-Q^{2}){\rm d}y\right|\leq\alpha_{2}.

Then we have

\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{0}>0\\
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\end{subarray}}\left\|u_{0}(\cdot)-\frac{\sigma_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}Q\left(\frac{\cdot-x_{0}}{\lambda_{0}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq\delta(\alpha_{2})\quad\mbox{where}\ \sigma_{0}\in\{-1,1\}.

Proof.

The proof is based on a standard variational argument. We refer to
[
40
, Lemma 1]
for the details of the proof.
∎

For the case of
E_{0}<0
, from Lemma
6.1
, we know that (Exit) is not possible for initial data with negative energy. Then, by (i) of Lemma
3.6
and Proposition
5.2
, we obtain

\lambda^{2}(t)|E_{0}|+\|\nabla\varepsilon(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim|b(t)|+\mathcal{N}_{0}(t)\rightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\ t\to T,

which implies the corresponding solution
u(t)
belongs to the Blow-up regime.

For the case of
E_{0}=0
, from Lemma
6.1
, we also know that (Exit) is not possible.
Then, the proof proceeds by contradiction. We assume that the corresponding solution
u(t)
belongs to the Soliton regime. A contradiction then follows from the subsequent discussion. We refer to
[
35
, §5]
for a similar proof.

From Proposition
5.2
, we can choose
t_{0}
large enough such that

|\lambda(t)-1|+|x_{1t}(t)-1|\leq\frac{1}{100},\quad\mbox{for all}\ t\geq t_{0}.

We define the smooth function
G_{0}\in C^{\infty}
with
G^{\prime}_{0}\leq 0
as follows,

G_{0}(x_{1})=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }x_{1}<-2,\\
1-(x_{1}+2)^{100}e^{-\frac{1}{x_{1}+2}},&\text{if }-2<x_{1}\leq-\frac{19}{10},\\
(x_{1}+1)^{100}e^{\frac{1}{x_{1}+1}},&\text{if }-\frac{11}{10}\leq x_{1}<-1,\\
0,&\text{if }x_{1}>-1.\end{cases}

We also define the smooth function
G_{1}\in C^{\infty}
with
G^{\prime}_{1}\leq 0
as follows,

G_{1}(x_{1})=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }x_{1}<-3,\\
1-(x_{1}+3)^{100}e^{-\frac{1}{x_{1}+3}},&\text{if }-3<x_{1}\leq-\frac{29}{10},\\
(x_{1}+2)^{100}e^{\frac{1}{x_{1}+2}},&\text{if }-\frac{21}{10}\leq x_{1}<-2,\\
0,&\text{if }x_{1}>-2.\end{cases}

From the fact that

\frac{{\rm d}x^{n}_{1}}{{\rm d}x_{1}}=nx_{1}^{n-1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{{\rm d}e^{-\frac{1}{x_{1}}}}{{\rm d}x_{1}}=\frac{1}{x_{1}^{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{x_{1}}},

we obtain

\displaystyle|G_{0}^{\prime\prime}|\lesssim|G_{0}^{\prime}|^{\frac{48}{49}},\quad|G_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}|\lesssim|G_{0}^{\prime}|^{\frac{47}{49}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad|G_{0}^{\prime}|\lesssim|G_{0}|^{\frac{49}{50}},

\displaystyle|G_{1}^{\prime\prime}|\lesssim|G_{1}^{\prime}|^{\frac{48}{49}},\quad|G_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}|\lesssim|G_{1}^{\prime}|^{\frac{47}{49}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad|G_{1}^{\prime}|\lesssim|G_{1}|^{\frac{49}{50}}.

For all
t_{0}\leq t_{1}\leq t
and
x_{0}\gg 1
, we set

\widetilde{G}_{0}(t,x_{1})=G_{0}(\widetilde{x})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{G}_{1}(t,x_{1})=G_{1}(\widetilde{x}),

where

L(t)=\frac{t-t_{1}}{4}+x_{0}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{x}=\frac{x_{1}-x_{1}(t)}{L(t)}.

Moreover, we denote

\displaystyle M_{x_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial_{x_{1}}u(t)|^{2}\widetilde{G}_{1}(t,x_{1}){\rm d}x,

\displaystyle E_{x_{0}}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla u(t)|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}|u(t)|^{4}\right)\widetilde{G}_{0}(t,x_{1}){\rm d}x.

We are in a position to complete the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

End of the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

We split the proof into the following three steps.

Step 1. Control of
E_{x_{0}}(t)
. By an elementary computation, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}{E}_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\left(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u\right)^{2}+\left(\partial_{x_{1}x_{2}}u\right)^{2}\right)G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla u(t)|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}|u(t)|^{4}\right)G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x})\widetilde{x}{\rm d}x

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\Delta u+u^{3}\right)^{2}G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x+\frac{1}{2L^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\partial_{x_{1}}u\right)^{2}G^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle-\frac{x_{1t}}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}|\nabla u(t)|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}|u(t)|^{4}\right)G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x+\frac{3}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}\left(\partial_{x_{1}}u\right)^{2}G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x.

First, from
{\rm{Supp}}\ G^{\prime}_{0}\subset[-2,-1]
and
x_{1t}\sim 1
, we have

-\frac{1}{8L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u(t)|G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x-\frac{x_{1t}}{2L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u(t)|G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x})\widetilde{x}{\rm d}x\geq\frac{1}{16L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u(t)|G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x.

Then, from Proposition
3.1
and §
5.2.1
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-1}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2}\right){\rm d}x_{1}{\rm d}x_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int^{-\frac{L}{\lambda(t)}}_{-\infty}\left(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|\nabla(bP\phi_{b})|^{2}+|\nabla\varepsilon|^{2}\right){\rm d}y_{1}{\rm d}y_{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{-\frac{L}{\lambda(t)}}\left(Q^{2}+(bP\phi_{b})^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\right){\rm d}y\lesssim e^{-\frac{t-t_{1}+x_{0}}{50}}+b(t)+\mathcal{N}_{0}(t)\to 0,\ \mbox{as}\ t\to\infty.

Based on the above estimate and the 2D Sobolev embedding, we obtain

\lim_{t\to\infty}E_{x_{0}}(t)=0,\quad\mbox{for any}\ x_{0}\gg 1.

On the other hand, it is easily checked that

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{-1}\left|\widetilde{G}_{0}\right|^{\frac{48}{49}},

(6.1)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\widetilde{G}_{0}\right|\lesssim L^{-\frac{50}{49}}\left|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}\right|^{\frac{48}{49}}

\displaystyle\mbox{and}\quad\left|\partial_{x_{1}}^{3}\widetilde{G}_{0}\right|\lesssim L^{-\frac{100}{49}}\left|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}\right|^{\frac{47}{49}}.

Therefore, based on a similar argument to the proof for Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\left\|u^{2}\sqrt{|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{-\frac{100}{49}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|^{\frac{45}{98}}{\rm d}x+L^{-\frac{50}{49}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|{\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{47}{98}}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-1}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2}\right){\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}\right)|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|{\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then, using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}\sqrt{|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|}{\rm d}x\right|\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-1}|\nabla u|^{2}{\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|{\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It follows from (
6.1
) that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}\left(\partial_{x_{1}}u\right)^{2}G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}\sqrt{|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|}{\rm d}x\right|\right)\left\|u^{2}\sqrt{|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L^{3}}+\frac{o(1)}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}\right)|{G}^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x})|{\rm d}x.

Here, we use the fact that

\lim_{t\to\infty}\left(L^{-1}(t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-1}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+u^{2}\right){\rm d}x\right)=0.

Then, based on a similar argument to the proof for Lemma
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{4}G_{0}^{\prime}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-1}u^{2}{\rm d}x\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}|{\rm d}x\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-1}u^{2}{\rm d}x\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}\left|\frac{\partial^{2}_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}}{\sqrt{\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}}}\right|^{2}{\rm d}x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L^{3}}+\frac{o(1)}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}\right)|{G}^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x})|{\rm d}x.

Combining the above estimates, one has

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}E_{x_{0}}(t)\geq-\frac{1}{20L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}\right)G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x-\frac{C}{L^{3}}.

Here,
C>1
is a universal constant independent with
L
. Integrating the above estimate over
[t_{1},t]
and then using the fact that
E_{x_{0}}(t)\to 0
as
t\to\infty
,

E_{x_{0}}(t_{1})-\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{L(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\partial^{2}_{x_{1}}u|^{2}+|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}\right)G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\lesssim\frac{1}{x^{2}_{0}}.

(6.2)

Step 2. Refined control of localized
\dot{H}^{1}
norm. First, using again (
6.1
) and a similar argument to the proof of Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{4}\widetilde{G}_{0}{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-1}u^{2}{\rm d}x\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}|\widetilde{G}_{0}|{\rm d}x\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-1}u^{2}{\rm d}x\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}\left|\frac{\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{0}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{G}_{0}}}\right|^{2}{\rm d}x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L^{2}}+o(1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}\widetilde{G}_{0}{\rm d}x\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{2}}+o(1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}\widetilde{G}_{0}{\rm d}x.

It follows from (
6.2
) that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-2}|\nabla u|^{2}{\rm d}x\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla u|^{2}\widetilde{G}_{0}{\rm d}x\lesssim E_{x_{0}}(t)+\frac{1}{x_{0}^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{x^{2}_{0}}.

(6.3)

Using again (
6.2
),

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}x_{0}}\left(\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right)G_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\right)

\displaystyle=-\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right)G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x})\frac{\widetilde{x}}{L}{\rm d}x{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\geq\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right)G^{\prime}_{0}(\widetilde{x})\frac{2}{L}{\rm d}x{\rm d}t\gtrsim-\frac{1}{x_{0}^{2}}.

Integrating the above estimate over
[x_{0},\infty)
, we see that

\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right)G_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x{\rm d}t\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}}.

(6.4)

Here, we use the fact that

\lim_{x_{0}\to\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right)G_{0}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x{\rm d}t=0.

Note that,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{-1}\left|\widetilde{G}_{1}\right|^{\frac{48}{49}},

(6.5)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}\widetilde{G}_{1}\right|\lesssim L^{-\frac{50}{49}}\left|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{1}\right|^{\frac{48}{49}}

\displaystyle\mbox{and}\quad\left|\partial_{x_{1}}^{3}\widetilde{G}_{1}\right|\lesssim L^{-\frac{100}{49}}\left|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{1}\right|^{\frac{47}{49}}.

By an elementary computation,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}M_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(3(\partial^{2}_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right)G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x-3\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{3}G_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2L^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x+\frac{3}{2L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x

\displaystyle-\frac{x_{1t}}{2L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x-\frac{1}{8L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x})\widetilde{x}{\rm d}x.

First, from Supp
G^{\prime}_{1}\subset[-3,-2]
and
x_{1t}\sim 1
, we have

-\frac{x_{1t}}{2L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x-\frac{1}{8L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x})\widetilde{x}{\rm d}x\geq-\frac{1}{20L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x.

Then, from (
5.9
) and (ii) of Lemma
5.1
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\left|\frac{1}{L^{3}(t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right|{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{3}}\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\left(e^{-\frac{t-t_{1}+x_{0}}{50}}+|b(t)|+\mathcal{N}_{0}(t)\right){\rm d}t\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{3}}.

Based on (
6.3
) and a similar argument to the one in the Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}\sqrt{|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{1}|}{\rm d}x\right|\right)\left\|u^{2}\sqrt{|\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{1}|}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L^{5}}+\frac{1}{x^{2}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}\right)|{G}^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x})|{\rm d}x.

It follows from (
6.4
) that

\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\left|\frac{1}{L}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right|{\rm d}t\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{3}}.

Then, using again (
6.5
) and a similar argument to the one in the Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}\sqrt{\widetilde{G}_{1}}{\rm d}x\right|\right)\left\|u^{2}\sqrt{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L^{5}}+\frac{1}{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+|\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}\right){G}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x.

Then, from (
6.3
), we see that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{4}G_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\widetilde{x}<-2}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}{\rm d}x\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((\partial^{2}_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right)G_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{\widetilde{x}<-2}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}{\rm d}x\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}\frac{\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{G}_{1}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{G}_{1}}}{\rm d}x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((\partial^{2}_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right){G}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x+\frac{1}{x^{2}_{0}L^{2}}.

Therefore, from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{3}G_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right|

(6.6)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}G_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{4}G_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((\partial^{2}_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right){G}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x+\frac{1}{L^{5}}+\frac{1}{x_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}L^{2}}.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}M_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim-\frac{1}{x_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((\partial^{2}_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right){G}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x-\frac{1}{L^{5}}-\frac{1}{x_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}L^{2}}.

Integrating the above estimate over
[t_{1},\infty)
, we obtain

\displaystyle M_{x_{0}}(t_{1})\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}}+\frac{1}{x_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{t_{1}}^{+\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left((\partial^{2}_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}\right){G}_{1}(\widetilde{x}){\rm d}x\right){\rm d}t\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}},

which implies

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\widetilde{x}<-3}|\partial_{x_{1}}u|^{2}\,{\rm d}x\leq G_{x_{0}}\lesssim\frac{1}{x_{0}^{\frac{5}{2}}}.

(6.7)

Step 3. Conclusion.
Using the Sobolev embedding with
x_{2}
fixed, for all
y_{0}\gg 1
,

\displaystyle|u(t,x_{1}(t)-2y_{0},x_{2})|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}(t)-y_{0}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}(t,x_{1},x_{2}){\rm d}x_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}(t)-y_{0}}u^{2}(t,x_{1},x_{2}){\rm d}x_{1}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

It follows from the Hölder’s inequality and (
6.7
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|u(t,x_{1}(t)-2y_{0},x_{2})|^{2}{\rm d}x_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\infty}^{x_{1}(t)-y_{0}}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}{\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}(t)-y_{0}}u^{2}{\rm d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{y_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}}.

Integrating the above estimate with respect to
y_{0}
over
[\frac{x_{0}}{2},\infty)
, we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}(t)-x_{0}}u^{2}\,{\rm d}x\lesssim\frac{1}{x^{\frac{1}{4}}_{0}}.

(6.8)

For any given
x_{0}\gg 1
, we split the
L_{2}
norm of
u
into the following two pieces,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u^{2}\,{\rm d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}(t)-x_{0}}u^{2}\,{\rm d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int^{\infty}_{x_{1}(t)-x_{0}}u^{2}\,{\rm d}x.

The first term above will go to
0
as
x_{0}\rightarrow\infty
by (
6.8
). Then, for the second term,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int^{\infty}_{x_{1}(t)-x_{0}}u^{2}\,{\rm d}x-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q^{2}\,{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int^{\infty}_{-\frac{x_{0}}{\lambda(t)}}|Q+b\phi_{b}P+\varepsilon|^{2}\,{\rm d}y-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q^{2}\,{\rm d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int^{\infty}_{-2x_{0}}\varepsilon^{2}\,{\rm d}y+|b|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi_{b}^{2}P^{2}\,{\rm d}y\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{x_{0}}{2}}Q^{2}\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int^{\infty}_{-2x_{0}}\varepsilon^{2}\,{\rm d}y+|b|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi_{b}^{2}P^{2}\,{\rm d}y\lesssim e^{\frac{10x_{0}}{B}}\mathcal{N}_{0}(t)+|b(t)|+e^{-\frac{x_{0}}{10}}.

We know
\mathcal{N}_{0}(t)
and
|b(t)|
go to
0
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. We can take a fixed
x_{0}
to make
e^{-\frac{x_{0}}{10}}
and
x^{-\frac{1}{4}}_{0}
as small as we want, and then pass
t\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, we obtain

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|u(t)|^{2}{\rm d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}Q^{2}{\rm d}x,

which leads a contradiction, and so the proof of the zero energy case is completed.
∎

Appendix A
Numerical computations

In this appendix, we provide some details for the numerical computations for the value of
\theta
. These numerical computations were carried out by Mathematica 13.1.

Recall that,
Q
is the unique nonnegative radial solution with exponential decay to the following second-order elliptic equation:

-\Delta Q+Q-Q^{3}=0\quad\mbox{on}\ \mathbb{R}^{2}.

Recall also that, we denote by
\Lambda
the scaling operator
\Lambda={\rm{Id}}+x\cdot\nabla
.

Our numerical computations are used to compute

\theta=2\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\big|\widehat{F}(\xi)\big|^{2}}{1+|\xi|^{2}}{\rm d}\xi\bigg)\bigg/\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\widehat{F}(\xi)\big|^{2}{\rm d}\xi\right).

Here, the functions
F
and
\widehat{F}
are given by

F(y_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Lambda Q(y_{1},y_{2}){\rm d}y_{1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{F}(\xi)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}F(y_{2})e^{-iy_{2}\xi}{\rm d}y_{2}.

A.1.
Numerical computations of
Q

We numerically compute
Q
in the polar coordinates

-R_{rr}-\frac{1}{r}R_{r}+R-R^{3}=0,

with
R_{r}(0)=0
and
R(r)\rightarrow 0
as
r\rightarrow 0
. To perform the numerical computation, we truncate the system to
r\in[0,L]
and set
R(L)=0
. Then we employ the non-spectral renormalization method to iterate and obtain an approximated solution. For full details, we refer to Section 3 of Chapter 28 in Fibich
[
10
]
which also contains references on the convergence of the non-spectral renormalization method. Due to the exponential decay of
Q
, the error caused by the truncation is very small. After obtaining the numerical solution in
r
, we use the standard interpolation to recover the numerical solution in the
(x_{1},x_{2})
coordinate.

A.2.
Fourier transforms

We use the default numerical integrations in Mathematica to integrate the numerical solution obtained above to find an approximation of
F
. Following this, we apply the FFT (Fast Fourier Transform) in Mathematica to compute the Fourier transform of the approximated
F
. It is necessary to renormalize the constants to align with our conventions of Fourier transforms; see the codes in the next section for details.

A.3.
Mathematica code

With these numerical computations, one has

We also plot the graph of
\widehat{F}(\xi)
with
L=20
and a grid size of
0.02
as a reference.

Picking
L=20
and the grid size to be
0.01
, we record

\theta=2\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\big|\widehat{F}(\xi)\big|^{2}}{1+|\xi|^{2}}{\rm d}\xi\bigg)\bigg/\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\widehat{F}(\xi)\big|^{2}{\rm d}\xi\right)\approx 1.66032.

For the sake of completeness, we provide the code for computations in Mathematica.
